\medterm{paenibacillus} paenibacillus is a microorganism or microbial group identified by its biologic and taxonomic features. Clinical significance depends on pathogenicity, exposure, the site involved, and antimicrobial susceptibility.

\medterm{paenibacillus alvei} paenibacillus alvei is a microorganism or microbial group identified by its biologic and taxonomic features. Clinical significance depends on pathogenicity, exposure, the site involved, and antimicrobial susceptibility.

\medterm{paenibacillus phoenicis} paenibacillus phoenicis is a microorganism or microbial group identified by its biologic and taxonomic features. Clinical significance depends on pathogenicity, exposure, the site involved, and antimicrobial susceptibility.

\medterm{paenibacillus polymyxa} paenibacillus polymyxa is a microorganism or microbial group identified by its biologic and taxonomic features. Clinical significance depends on pathogenicity, exposure, the site involved, and antimicrobial susceptibility.

\medterm{pagetoid reticulosis} A rare localized variant of cutaneous T-cell lymphoma with solitary slowly enlarging scaly plaques containing atypical pagetoid lymphocytes.

\medterm{pain perception} pain perception describes a clinical disorder or finding in the body system or process named by the term. Evaluation identifies the underlying mechanism, affected site, severity, and clinically relevant complications.

\medterm{pain threshold} pain threshold describes a clinical disorder or finding in the body system or process named by the term. Evaluation identifies the underlying mechanism, affected site, severity, and clinically relevant complications.

\medterm{palatal muscle} Muscle of the soft palate that elevates or tenses the palate and helps separate the nasal and oral cavities during swallowing and speech.

\medterm{palate neoplasm} palate neoplasm describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{palatine tonsil} An anatomical structure, tissue, or physiologic process with the location or function indicated by the term; disease or injury changes its expected findings.

\medterm{palatorrhaphy} Palatorrhaphy is surgical repair of a cleft or defect of the palate to restore separation between the oral and nasal cavities and improve feeding and speech.

\medterm{palifermin} A medicine or pharmacologic agent used for the condition or pathway indicated by its name; mechanism, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{palivizumab} palivizumab is a medication or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{palladium} palladium is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{palm oil} Palm oil is an edible plant oil high in palmitic and oleic acids; its nutritional effects depend on total dietary pattern and the fats it replaces.

\medterm{palmar reflex} The palmar reflex is an infant response in which stroking the palm causes finger flexion; persistence or asymmetry beyond the expected age may indicate neurologic dysfunction.

\medterm{palmitic acid} A 16-carbon saturated fatty acid common in dietary and stored fats; excess saturated-fat intake can contribute to adverse lipid profiles, although the body also synthesizes it normally.

\medterm{palmoplantar hyperkeratosis} Excessive thickening of the skin on the palms and soles, inherited or acquired, causing calluses, fissures, pain, impaired walking, and sometimes infection.

\medterm{palonosetron} A medicine or pharmacologic agent used for the condition or pathway indicated by its name; mechanism, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{palonosetron hydrochloride} A long-acting 5-HT3 serotonin-receptor antagonist used to prevent nausea and vomiting caused by chemotherapy, surgery, or selected other treatments.

\medterm{palpable} Palpable means detectable by touch during physical examination, such as a mass, enlarged organ, lymph node, pulse, tenderness, or abnormal surface.

\medterm{palsy} Weakness or paralysis of a body part caused by dysfunction of its motor nerves, muscles, spinal pathways, or brain; the distribution and onset help identify the cause.

\medterm{pamidronic acid} An intravenous bisphosphonate that inhibits osteoclast-mediated bone resorption and is used for selected hypercalcemia, bone complications of cancer, and Paget disease.

\medterm{pan troglodyte} Pan troglodytes is the scientific name for the common chimpanzee, a great ape and close evolutionary relative of humans used in behavioral and biomedical research.

\medterm{panax} Panax is a plant genus that includes ginseng species whose roots are used in traditional products; composition, evidence, interactions, and adverse effects vary by preparation.

\medterm{pancreas enzyme} An enzyme produced by the pancreas, such as amylase, lipase, or proteases, that helps digest carbohydrate, fat, or protein in the small intestine.

\medterm{pancreas infection} pancreas infection describes an infectious or toxic process involving the site or exposure named by the term. Diagnosis considers the cause, route of exposure, affected tissues, severity, and host response.

\medterm{pancreas neoplasm} pancreas neoplasm describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{pancreas transplantation} pancreas transplantation is a medical procedure, treatment, device, or care topic; indications, benefits, risks, and follow-up depend on the patient and clinical goal.

\medterm{pancreatic carcinoma} pancreatic carcinoma describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{pancreatic diseases} pancreatic diseases describes a clinical disorder or finding in the body system or process named by the term. Evaluation identifies the underlying mechanism, affected site, severity, and clinically relevant complications.

\medterm{pancreatic fistula} A pancreatic fistula is an abnormal passage through which pancreatic juice leaks from the pancreas, often after trauma or surgery, causing fluid collections, ascites, or skin drainage.

\medterm{pancreatic hormones} Hormones produced by pancreatic islet cells, including insulin, glucagon, somatostatin, and pancreatic polypeptide, which regulate glucose, digestion, and energy metabolism.

\medterm{pancreatic islet} A pancreatic islet is a cluster of endocrine cells that releases hormones such as insulin, glucagon, and somatostatin to regulate blood glucose and digestion.

\medterm{pancreatic juice} Pancreatic juice is an alkaline digestive secretion containing bicarbonate and enzymes such as amylase, lipase, and proteases, delivered into the duodenum.

\medterm{pancreaticoduodenectomy} pancreaticoduodenectomy is used in relation to pancreaticoduodenectomy during assessment, diagnosis, treatment, monitoring, or patient support.

\medterm{pancrelipase} Pancrelipase is a mixture of pancreatic digestive enzymes used as replacement therapy for exocrine pancreatic insufficiency, including that caused by cystic fibrosis or pancreatic disease.

\medterm{pancuronium} Pancuronium is a long-acting nondepolarizing neuromuscular blocker that prevents skeletal-muscle contraction during anesthesia or ventilation; it must be given with airway support.

\medterm{pandoraea} pandoraea identifies pandoraea, a biological taxon or infectious-disease concept whose clinical importance depends on the organism, host, and site involved.

\medterm{pargyline} Pargyline is an irreversible monoamine-oxidase inhibitor formerly used for hypertension; it is now mainly of historical or research interest because of interactions and adverse effects.

\medterm{paricalcitol} Paricalcitol is a vitamin-D receptor activator used to reduce secondary hyperparathyroidism in selected chronic kidney disease, with monitoring for calcium and phosphate changes.

\medterm{parietal pleura} An anatomical structure, tissue, or physiologic process with the location or function indicated by the term; disease or injury changes its expected findings.

\medterm{parinaric acid} A conjugated polyunsaturated fatty acid used as a fluorescent probe of lipid membranes and studied in oxidation and lipid-chemistry research.

\medterm{parity} Parity is the number of pregnancies that reached a viable gestational age, regardless of whether the infants were born alive or stillborn; it differs from gravidity.

\medterm{paromomycin} paromomycin is a medication or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{parosteal osteosarcoma} parosteal osteosarcoma describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{parotid neoplasm} parotid neoplasm describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{parotidectomy} parotidectomy is used in relation to parotidectomy during assessment, diagnosis, treatment, monitoring, or patient support.

\medterm{paroxetine} Paroxetine is a selective serotonin-reuptake inhibitor used for depression, anxiety disorders, obsessive-compulsive disorder, PTSD, and some other conditions; sexual dysfunction, withdrawal symptoms, serotonin syndrome, and pregnancy-related risks require consideration.

\medterm{paroxetine hydrochloride} paroxetine hydrochloride is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{pars compacta} The substantia nigra pars compacta is a midbrain region containing dopamine-producing neurons whose loss contributes to the motor features of Parkinson disease.

\medterm{pars planitis} A form of intermediate uveitis involving inflammatory exudates near the pars plana, causing floaters, blurred vision, and sometimes macular edema or retinal complications.

\medterm{parthenogenesis} Development of an embryo from an unfertilized egg; it occurs naturally in some animals and plants and is not a normal mechanism of human reproduction.

\medterm{partial} Partial means incomplete or involving only a portion, such as partial loss, partial response, partial thickness, or partial seizure; the specific meaning depends on context.

\medterm{partial cystectomy} partial cystectomy is used in relation to partial cystectomy during assessment, diagnosis, treatment, monitoring, or patient support.

\medterm{partial hysterectomy} partial hysterectomy is used in relation to partial hysterectomy during assessment, diagnosis, treatment, monitoring, or patient support.

\medterm{partial nephrectomy} partial nephrectomy is used in relation to partial nephrectomy during assessment, diagnosis, treatment, monitoring, or patient support.

\medterm{partial remission} A reduction in signs, symptoms, or measurable disease burden that does not meet criteria for complete remission; the definition depends on the disease and response criteria used.

\medterm{participation} A person’s involvement in life situations and activities, a central concept in rehabilitation and the International Classification of Functioning that extends beyond impairment alone.

\medterm{particle size} Particle size is the diameter or size distribution of particles in a powder, suspension, aerosol, or formulation, influencing dissolution, absorption, stability, and delivery.

\medterm{parvimonas micra} parvimonas micra identifies parvimonas micra, a biological taxon or infectious-disease concept whose clinical importance depends on the organism, host, and site involved.

\medterm{parvoviridae} parvoviridae identifies parvoviridae, a biological taxon or infectious-disease concept whose clinical importance depends on the organism, host, and site involved.

\medterm{parvovirus} parvovirus is a virus or viral group classified by its genetic and biologic features. Clinical importance depends on the strain, host, route of transmission, tissue tropism, and ability to cause disease.

\medterm{passive immunization} Protection produced by giving preformed antibodies, such as immunoglobulin or antivenom, which acts quickly but is temporary because the recipient does not generate durable immune memory.

\medterm{paste} A paste is a semisolid preparation or material containing finely divided solids in a liquid or base, used in medicines, dentistry, skin care, and laboratory work.

\medterm{pasteurella} pasteurella identifies pasteurella, a biological taxon or infectious-disease concept whose clinical importance depends on the organism, host, and site involved.

\medterm{pasteurella aerogenes} pasteurella aerogenes identifies pasteurella aerogenes, a biological taxon or infectious-disease concept whose clinical importance depends on the organism, host, and site involved.

\medterm{pasteurella bettyae} pasteurella bettyae identifies pasteurella bettyae, a biological taxon or infectious-disease concept whose clinical importance depends on the organism, host, and site involved.

\medterm{pasteurella canis} pasteurella canis identifies pasteurella canis, a biological taxon or infectious-disease concept whose clinical importance depends on the organism, host, and site involved.

\medterm{pasteurella pneumotropica} pasteurella pneumotropica identifies pasteurella pneumotropica, a biological taxon or infectious-disease concept whose clinical importance depends on the organism, host, and site involved.

\medterm{pasteurellaceae} pasteurellaceae identifies pasteurellaceae, a biological taxon or infectious-disease concept whose clinical importance depends on the organism, host, and site involved.

\medterm{patellar dislocation} Complete displacement of the kneecap from the femoral groove, usually laterally, causing acute pain, deformity, swelling, and difficulty extending the knee.

\medterm{patent urachus} A patent urachus is persistence of the fetal channel between the bladder and umbilicus after birth, allowing urine to drain from the umbilicus and predisposing to infection.

\medterm{paternal behavior} Paternal behavior includes caregiving, protection, bonding, and other behaviors by a father or male caregiver; it is shaped by biology, experience, culture, and circumstances.

\medterm{pathologic dilatation} Abnormal widening of a hollow organ, duct, blood vessel, or other structure beyond its expected size because of obstruction, weakness, pressure, or disease.

\medterm{pathologic ossification} Abnormal formation of bone in soft tissue or in a location where bone should not develop, as in heterotopic ossification after trauma, neurologic injury, or surgery.

\medterm{pathologic process} A pathologic process is an abnormal biological change that produces disease, such as infection, inflammation, ischemia, degeneration, abnormal growth, or immune injury.

\medterm{pathologic response} A pathologic response is an abnormal reaction of cells, tissues, or organs to injury, infection, immune activation, toxins, or other stressors that contributes to disease.

\medterm{pathological fracture} pathological fracture describes a clinical disorder or finding in the body system or process named by the term. Evaluation identifies the underlying mechanism, affected site, severity, and clinically relevant complications.

\medterm{pathologist} A pathologist is a physician who diagnoses disease by examining tissues, cells, blood, or body fluids and may guide laboratory testing, cancer classification, and treatment decisions.

\medterm{patient care} patient care is a medical procedure, treatment, device, or care topic; indications, benefits, risks, and follow-up depend on the patient and clinical goal.

\medterm{patient discharge} patient discharge is a medical procedure, treatment, device, or care topic; indications, benefits, risks, and follow-up depend on the patient and clinical goal.

\medterm{patient observation} Systematic monitoring of a patient’s symptoms, vital signs, examination findings, behavior, and response to treatment to detect deterioration or guide management.

\medterm{patient rights} Ethical and legal protections for people receiving care, including informed consent, privacy, access to information, respectful treatment, participation in decisions, and refusal of treatment where lawful.

\medterm{paxillin} Paxillin is a focal-adhesion adaptor protein that links integrin signaling to the actin cytoskeleton and helps regulate cell adhesion, migration, and mechanical sensing.

\medterm{pazopanib} pazopanib is a medication or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{peanut oil} Peanut oil is oil extracted from peanuts; refined oil usually contains little allergenic protein, whereas unrefined products may trigger reactions in susceptible people.

\medterm{peau d'orange} Peau d’orange is dimpled skin resembling an orange peel, caused by edema tethered around hair follicles; it can occur with breast cancer, infection, or lymphatic obstruction.

\medterm{pectin} Pectin is a soluble plant fiber that forms gels and may alter stool consistency and absorption; it is used in foods, formulations, and some nutritional products.

\medterm{pectinate line} The pectinate line is the junction in the anal canal between regions with different embryologic origin, blood supply, lymphatic drainage, sensation, and venous anatomy.

\medterm{pectoral girdle} The pectoral girdle consists of the clavicles and scapulae, attaching the upper limbs to the trunk and providing broad surfaces for shoulder and arm movement.

\medterm{pectoralis muscles} The pectoralis major and minor muscles of the chest, which move and stabilize the shoulder and assist with movements such as pushing, adduction, and breathing.

\medterm{pediatric AIDS} Pediatric AIDS is advanced HIV infection in a child, with severe immune suppression or AIDS-defining illness; antiretroviral therapy and prevention of opportunistic infection are essential.

\medterm{pediatric nursing} Pediatric nursing provides age-appropriate care to infants, children, and adolescents, accounting for growth, development, family involvement, communication, safety, and medication differences.

\medterm{pediatric obesity} Excess body fat in a child or adolescent defined using age- and sex-specific BMI percentiles, associated with metabolic, orthopedic, respiratory, psychosocial, and cardiovascular risks.

\medterm{pediococcus} pediococcus is a microorganism or microbial group identified by its biologic and taxonomic features. Clinical significance depends on pathogenicity, exposure, the site involved, and antimicrobial susceptibility.

\medterm{pediococcus pentosaceus} pediococcus pentosaceus is a microorganism or microbial group identified by its biologic and taxonomic features. Clinical significance depends on pathogenicity, exposure, the site involved, and antimicrobial susceptibility.

\medterm{pedophilia} Pedophilia is a psychiatric term for a persistent sexual interest in prepubescent children; sexual contact with a child is abuse and illegal, and safety-focused professional help is required.

\medterm{pedunculate} Pedunculate describes a structure, lesion, or polyp attached by a narrow stalk rather than a broad base.

\medterm{peer review} Peer review is evaluation of research, clinical work, or scholarly writing by qualified independent reviewers to assess methods, evidence, clarity, and significance.

\medterm{pefloxacin} A medicine or pharmacologic agent used for the condition or pathway indicated by its name; mechanism, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{pegargiminase} A medicine or pharmacologic agent used for the condition or pathway indicated by its name; mechanism, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{pegaspargase} A medicine or pharmacologic agent used for the condition or pathway indicated by its name; mechanism, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{pegdinetanib} pegdinetanib is a medication or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{pegfilgrastim} A medicine or pharmacologic agent used for the condition or pathway indicated by its name; mechanism, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{peldesine} Peldesine is an investigational sphingosine-1-phosphate lyase inhibitor studied for effects on immune-cell trafficking and inflammatory or autoimmune disease.

\medterm{pelitrexol} Pelitrexol is an investigational antifolate drug that inhibits thymidylate synthase and has been studied as an antineoplastic agent.

\medterm{pellet} A pellet is a small compacted mass of medication, material, or tissue; drug pellets may be formulated for implantation or controlled release.

\medterm{pelvic bone} The ring of bones formed by the paired hip bones, sacrum, and coccyx that supports the trunk, protects pelvic organs, and transmits weight to the lower limbs.

\medterm{pelvic diaphragm} An anatomical structure, tissue, or physiologic process with the location or function indicated by the term; disease or injury changes its expected findings.

\medterm{pelvic examination} Clinical inspection and palpation of the external genitalia, vagina, cervix, uterus, and adnexa to evaluate bleeding, pain, discharge, lesions, pregnancy-related findings, or infection.

\medterm{pelvic exenteration} Extensive surgery removing selected pelvic organs and surrounding tissues, reserved for carefully selected locally advanced or recurrent cancers when less radical treatment is inadequate.

\medterm{pelvic girdle} An anatomical structure, tissue, or physiologic process with the location or function indicated by the term; disease or injury changes its expected findings.

\medterm{pelvic infection} pelvic infection describes an infectious or toxic process involving the site or exposure named by the term. Diagnosis considers the cause, route of exposure, affected tissues, severity, and host response.

\medterm{pelvic kidney} A kidney that remains in the pelvis rather than ascending to its usual lumbar position during development; it may be asymptomatic or associated with abnormal drainage, stones, infection, or vascular anatomy.

\medterm{pelvic lymphadenectomy} pelvic lymphadenectomy is used in relation to pelvic lymphadenectomy during assessment, diagnosis, treatment, monitoring, or patient support.

\medterm{pelvic mass} An anatomical structure, tissue, or physiologic process with the location or function indicated by the term; disease or injury changes its expected findings.

\medterm{pelvic neoplasm} pelvic neoplasm describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{pelvic viscus} An anatomical structure, tissue, or physiologic process with the location or function indicated by the term; disease or injury changes its expected findings.

\medterm{pemetrexed} Pemetrexed is a folate-antimetabolite chemotherapy drug that inhibits several enzymes involved in nucleotide synthesis, used especially for mesothelioma and nonsquamous non-small-cell lung cancer; folic acid and vitamin B12 reduce toxicity.

\medterm{pemetrexed disodium} pemetrexed disodium is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{pemoline} Pemoline is a stimulant formerly used for attention-deficit disorders; serious liver toxicity led to withdrawal or severe restriction in many countries.

\medterm{penbutolol} penbutolol is a medication or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{penclomedine} Penclomedine is an investigational antineoplastic compound studied for cytotoxic effects against cancer cells and is not a routine clinical medicine.

\medterm{pendulous} Pendulous means hanging loosely from a supporting structure, as in a pedunculated lesion, drooping tissue, or an organ suspended by a stalk.

\medterm{penicillamine} A medicine or pharmacologic agent used for the condition or pathway indicated by its name; mechanism, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{penicillin} Penicillin is a beta-lactam antibiotic that binds penicillin-binding proteins and inhibits bacterial cell-wall cross-linking; allergy, including anaphylaxis, and antimicrobial resistance are important considerations.

\medterm{penicillin G} penicillin G is a medication or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{penicillin V} penicillin V is a medication or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{penicillium} penicillium is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{penile disease} penile disease describes a clinical disorder or finding in the body system or process named by the term. Evaluation identifies the underlying mechanism, affected site, severity, and clinically relevant complications.

\medterm{penile erection} Rigidity of the penis caused by increased arterial inflow and reduced venous outflow after sexual or reflex stimulation; persistent difficulty obtaining or maintaining it is erectile dysfunction.

\medterm{penile infection} penile infection describes an infectious or toxic process involving the site or exposure named by the term. Diagnosis considers the cause, route of exposure, affected tissues, severity, and host response.

\medterm{penile leukoplakia} A disease, symptom, syndrome, or clinical finding involving the body system or process named by the term; characteristic features, causes, severity, and complications guide diagnosis and management.

\medterm{penis carcinoma} penis carcinoma describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{penoscrotal transposition} A congenital difference in which the penis and scrotum are positioned abnormally relative to each other, often associated with hypospadias or other genital and urinary anomalies.

\medterm{pentadecanoic acid} A 15-carbon saturated fatty acid found in small amounts in dairy and ruminant fats and used in nutritional and lipid-metabolism research.

\medterm{pentamidine} Pentamidine is an antimicrobial drug used for Pneumocystis jirovecii pneumonia and selected protozoal infections; hypotension, hypoglycaemia or hyperglycaemia, renal injury, pancreatitis, and arrhythmias may occur.

\medterm{pentamidine isethionate} An antiprotozoal and antimicrobial drug used in selected cases of Pneumocystis pneumonia, leishmaniasis, trypanosomiasis, and other infections; toxicity includes hypoglycemia, kidney injury, and arrhythmia.

\medterm{pentatrichomonas hominis} Pentatrichomonas hominis is a flagellated intestinal protozoan generally regarded as nonpathogenic; finding it in stool usually indicates exposure to fecal contamination.

\medterm{pentetic acid} A chelating compound that binds metal ions and is used in selected radiopharmaceuticals, contrast agents, or laboratory applications.

\medterm{pentobarbital} A medicine or pharmacologic agent used for the condition or pathway indicated by its name; mechanism, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{pentosan polysulfate} pentosan polysulfate is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{pentose} A pentose is a five-carbon sugar, including ribose in RNA and deoxyribose in DNA, and participates in nucleotide and carbohydrate metabolism.

\medterm{pentoxifylline} pentoxifylline describes an infectious or toxic process involving the site or exposure named by the term. Diagnosis considers the cause, route of exposure, affected tissues, severity, and host response.

\medterm{pentylenetetrazole} pentylenetetrazole is a medication or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{peplomycin} peplomycin is a medication or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{pepsin A} Pepsin A is a stomach protease activated by acid that begins digestion of dietary proteins into smaller peptides.

\medterm{pepsinogen} Pepsinogen is the inactive precursor secreted by gastric chief cells that is converted to pepsin in acidic stomach contents.

\medterm{peptic esophagitis} Inflammation and injury of the esophageal lining caused mainly by repeated gastroesophageal reflux of acid or bile, producing heartburn, chest discomfort, swallowing pain, or strictures.

\medterm{peptichemio} peptichemio is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{peptide} peptide is a defined chemical or biologic substance involved in peptide and studied in physiology, pharmacology, laboratory medicine, or treatment.

\medterm{peptide biosynthesis} peptide biosynthesis concerns peptide biosynthesis, a biologic or physiologic concept used in research, laboratory medicine, and the explanation of disease.

\medterm{peptide hormone} A hormone made of amino acids that binds a cell-surface receptor and regulates processes such as metabolism, growth, reproduction, stress, and fluid balance.

\medterm{peptide library} peptide library concerns peptide library, a biologic or physiologic concept used in research, laboratory medicine, and the explanation of disease.

\medterm{peptide metabolism} peptide metabolism concerns peptide metabolism, a biologic or physiologic concept used in research, laboratory medicine, and the explanation of disease.

\medterm{peptide transport} peptide transport concerns peptide transport, a biologic or physiologic concept used in research, laboratory medicine, and the explanation of disease.

\medterm{peptide yy} peptide yy concerns peptide yy, a biologic or physiologic concept used in research, laboratory medicine, and the explanation of disease.

\medterm{peptidoglycan} peptidoglycan is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{peptococcaceae} peptococcaceae is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{peptococcus} peptococcus is a biological organism or infectious agent; its clinical importance depends on the species, host, site, exposure, and evidence of infection or colonization.

\medterm{peptococcus niger} peptococcus niger is a biological organism or infectious agent; its clinical importance depends on the species, host, site, exposure, and evidence of infection or colonization.

\medterm{peptoniphilus} peptoniphilus is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{peptoniphilus harei} peptoniphilus harei is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{peptoniphilus indolicus} peptoniphilus indolicus is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{peptostreptococcus} peptostreptococcus is a biological organism or infectious agent; its clinical importance depends on the species, host, site, exposure, and evidence of infection or colonization.

\medterm{peracetic acid} A strong oxidizing disinfectant and sterilant used for equipment and surfaces; concentrated exposure irritates or burns skin, eyes, and airways.

\medterm{peracute} Peracute describes disease or a clinical event that develops extremely rapidly, often over minutes to hours, with little time for adaptation or diagnosis.

\medterm{perception} The brain’s process of organizing and interpreting sensory information into an experience of objects, events, body sensations, or the environment.

\medterm{perceptual disorder} perceptual disorder describes a clinical disorder or finding in the body system or process named by the term. Evaluation identifies the underlying mechanism, affected site, severity, and clinically relevant complications.

\medterm{perceptual distortion} An inaccurate or altered interpretation of a real sensory stimulus, which may occur with neurologic disease, psychiatric illness, drugs, migraine, seizures, or sensory impairment.

\medterm{perceptual masking} Reduction in the ability to detect or identify one sensory stimulus because another stimulus overlaps it in time, space, or processing.

\medterm{percutaneous discectomy} percutaneous discectomy is used in relation to percutaneous discectomy during assessment, diagnosis, treatment, monitoring, or patient support.

\medterm{perforant pathway} An anatomical structure, tissue, or physiologic process with the location or function indicated by the term; disease or injury changes its expected findings.

\medterm{performance} Performance describes how effectively a person, organ, treatment, test, or system carries out a defined task; the relevant measure depends on the clinical context.

\medterm{performed diagnosis} A diagnosis established after clinical assessment and appropriate testing identifies the condition explaining a patient’s findings; it should be distinguished from a symptom or provisional impression.

\medterm{performed histopathology} performed histopathology is a diagnostic test, specimen, imaging method, score, instrument, or measurement used to answer a clinical or laboratory question; results must be interpreted with the method and reference range.

\medterm{performed procedure} performed procedure is a medical procedure, treatment, device, or care topic; indications, benefits, risks, and follow-up depend on the patient and clinical goal.

\medterm{perfusion culture} A laboratory culture system in which cells or tissue receive continuously renewed nutrient medium, supporting controlled study of growth, metabolism, infection, or drug effects.

\medterm{pergolide} A medicine or pharmacologic agent used for the condition or pathway indicated by its name; mechanism, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{perianal} Located around the anus; the term describes the skin, glands, pain, inflammation, abscesses, fistulas, and other findings in that region.

\medterm{perianal abscess} A localized collection of pus near the anus caused by infected anal glands or surrounding tissue, producing severe throbbing pain, swelling, fever, and sometimes a fistula.

\medterm{perianal glands} Small glands near the anus that contribute secretions to the local tissue; obstruction or infection can contribute to perianal abscesses and fistulas.

\medterm{periarteritis nodosa} An older name for polyarteritis nodosa, a necrotizing vasculitis of medium-sized arteries that can cause skin, nerve, muscle, kidney, and abdominal ischemic symptoms without typical glomerulonephritis.

\medterm{periarticular} Periarticular means located around a joint, including nearby tendons, bursae, ligaments, capsule, and soft tissues rather than the joint surface itself.

\medterm{pericardial mesothelioma} An anatomical structure, tissue, or physiologic process with the location or function indicated by the term; disease or injury changes its expected findings.

\medterm{pericardial stripping} An anatomical structure, tissue, or physiologic process with the location or function indicated by the term; disease or injury changes its expected findings.

\medterm{pericardial wall} An anatomical structure, tissue, or physiologic process with the location or function indicated by the term; disease or injury changes its expected findings.

\medterm{pericardiectomy} pericardiectomy is used in relation to pericardiectomy during assessment, diagnosis, treatment, monitoring, or patient support.

\medterm{pericardiostomy} An anatomical structure, tissue, or physiologic process with the location or function indicated by the term; disease or injury changes its expected findings.

\medterm{pericytes} Pericytes are contractile support cells wrapped around small blood vessels that help regulate capillary stability, blood flow, repair, and the blood-brain barrier.

\medterm{perifosine} Perifosine is an investigational alkylphospholipid that affects cell-membrane signaling, including AKT pathways, and has been studied in cancer treatment.

\medterm{perillyl alcohol} Perillyl alcohol is a monoterpene found in some plant oils and studied experimentally for effects on cell signaling and cancer growth.

\medterm{perilymph} An anatomical structure, tissue, or physiologic process with the location or function indicated by the term; disease or injury changes its expected findings.

\medterm{perimenopausal} Perimenopausal describes the years of hormonal and menstrual transition before and around menopause, when cycles, bleeding, hot flashes, sleep, and fertility may change.

\medterm{perinatal death} A disease, symptom, syndrome, or clinical finding involving the body system or process named by the term; characteristic features, causes, severity, and complications guide diagnosis and management.

\medterm{perindopril} perindopril is a medication or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{perineural} Perineural means around a nerve; the term may describe normal connective tissue, inflammation, tumor spread, or imaging findings surrounding a nerve.

\medterm{perineuronal satellitosis} Clustering of glial cells around a neuron, seen microscopically in some brain tumors, inflammatory conditions, and reactive processes; its significance depends on the surrounding pathology.

\medterm{perinuclear space} The narrow compartment between the inner and outer nuclear membranes, continuous with the endoplasmic-reticulum lumen and involved in nuclear-envelope structure.

\medterm{periodic acid} An oxidizing acid used in histochemistry, especially the periodic-acid--Schiff reaction, which highlights carbohydrates, mucosubstances, fungi, and basement membranes.

\medterm{periodontal cyst} A periodontal cyst is a fluid-filled epithelial-lined lesion near the tooth-supporting tissues, with effects ranging from incidental discovery to local bone destruction.

\medterm{periodontal dressing} periodontal dressing is a procedure, device, treatment, or care intervention; indications, benefits, risks, and follow-up depend on the patient and clinical goal.

\medterm{periodontist} A periodontist is a dentist specializing in prevention, diagnosis, and treatment of gum disease and supporting tissues, including periodontal surgery and implants.

\medterm{periodontium} The periodontium is the tooth-supporting apparatus: gingiva, periodontal ligament, cementum, and alveolar bone.

\medterm{periodontium disease} periodontium disease describes a clinical disorder or finding in the body system or process named by the term. Evaluation identifies the underlying mechanism, affected site, severity, and clinically relevant complications.

\medterm{periorbital edema} periorbital edema describes a clinical disorder or finding in the body system or process named by the term. Evaluation identifies the underlying mechanism, affected site, severity, and clinically relevant complications.

\medterm{periorbital infection} periorbital infection describes an infectious or toxic process involving the site or exposure named by the term. Diagnosis considers the cause, route of exposure, affected tissues, severity, and host response.

\medterm{periostitis} Inflammation of the periosteum, the vascular connective-tissue layer covering bone, causing localized pain and tenderness from trauma, infection, repetitive stress, or inflammatory disease.

\medterm{peripheral blood} Peripheral blood is blood obtained from a vein or capillary rather than directly from an organ or marrow, used for counts, smears, chemistry, and other testing.

\medterm{peripheral ischemia} Inadequate arterial blood flow to an arm, leg, digit, or other peripheral tissue, causing pain, pallor, coolness, weak pulses, numbness, ulcers, or tissue loss.

\medterm{peripheral nerve} A nerve outside the brain and spinal cord that carries motor, sensory, and autonomic signals between the central nervous system and the rest of the body.

\medterm{periplasm} The periplasm is the space between the inner and outer membranes of Gram-negative bacteria, containing enzymes, transport proteins, and structural components.

\medterm{perirenal hematoma} A disease, symptom, syndrome, or clinical finding involving the body system or process named by the term; characteristic features, causes, severity, and complications guide diagnosis and management.

\medterm{peritoneal} Peritoneal means relating to the peritoneum, the thin membrane lining the abdominal cavity and covering many abdominal organs.

\medterm{peritoneal cavity} The potential space between the parietal and visceral peritoneum surrounding abdominal and pelvic organs; fluid, blood, pus, or tumor cells can accumulate there.

\medterm{peritoneal diseases} peritoneal diseases describes a clinical disorder or finding in the body system or process named by the term. Evaluation identifies the underlying mechanism, affected site, severity, and clinically relevant complications.

\medterm{peritoneal fluid} Peritoneal fluid is the small amount of lubricating fluid in the abdominal cavity; excess or infected fluid may cause ascites, peritonitis, or diagnostic findings.

\medterm{peritoneal lavage} Peritoneal lavage is washing or sampling the abdominal cavity with sterile fluid, historically used to detect internal bleeding or contamination and sometimes used therapeutically.

\medterm{peritoneal necrosis} Death of peritoneal tissue caused by ischemia, severe infection, inflammation, trauma, or tumor, potentially leading to peritonitis, adhesions, sepsis, or organ failure.

\medterm{peritoneal sac} The peritoneal sac is the potential space enclosed by the peritoneum, containing lubricating fluid and allowing abdominal organs to move with minimal friction.

\medterm{peritoneum neoplasm} peritoneum neoplasm describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{perivitelline space} The perivitelline space is the narrow region between an egg’s plasma membrane and surrounding vitelline or zona material, important in fertilization and early development.

\medterm{permease} A permease is a membrane transport protein that facilitates movement of a specific molecule or ion across a cell membrane.

\medterm{permethrin} Permethrin is a synthetic pyrethroid insecticide used for scabies and lice and for pest control; skin irritation and inappropriate environmental exposure are possible concerns.

\medterm{permissiveness} Permissiveness is the ability of a cell or host environment to allow a biological process, such as viral replication, gene expression, or tissue response, to occur.

\medterm{peroneal nerve} The common fibular nerve, a branch of the sciatic nerve that winds around the fibular neck and supplies sensation and muscles responsible for ankle dorsiflexion and eversion.

\medterm{peroxidase} peroxidase is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{peroxisomal disorder} peroxisomal disorder describes a clinical disorder or finding in the body system or process named by the term. Evaluation identifies the underlying mechanism, affected site, severity, and clinically relevant complications.

\medterm{peroxisome proliferation} An increase in the number or size of peroxisomes, organelles involved in fatty-acid oxidation and reactive-oxygen metabolism, triggered by selected chemicals, hormones, or metabolic states.

\medterm{perphenazine} Perphenazine is a first-generation antipsychotic used for selected psychotic disorders or severe nausea; movement disorders, sedation, and other adverse effects require monitoring.

\medterm{perseveration} Involuntary repetition of a word, idea, movement, or response after the appropriate stimulus has changed, associated with frontal or basal-ganglia disease, aphasia, delirium, or psychiatric illness.

\medterm{persistent cloaca} Persistent cloaca is a congenital malformation in which the rectum, vagina, and urinary tract join into one channel, requiring specialist evaluation and reconstructive care.

\medterm{person} A person is an individual human being; clinical care considers the person’s biology, symptoms, preferences, capacity, relationships, and social circumstances.

\medterm{person name} A person name is an identifying name associated with an individual; accurate identification is essential for safe records, testing, prescribing, and communication.

\medterm{person with disability} A person with disability has a long-term physical, sensory, intellectual, or mental-health impairment that may interact with barriers to limit participation; care should be accessible and person-centered.

\medterm{personality} Personality is the relatively enduring pattern of thoughts, emotions, motivations, and behaviors through which a person experiences and responds to the world.

\medterm{personality change} Personality change is a new or marked alteration in behavior, emotional responses, judgment, or interpersonal style, which may reflect neurologic disease, psychiatric illness, substances, or medical conditions.

\medterm{personality test} A psychological assessment using structured questions or stimuli to describe enduring traits, patterns, or possible personality pathology; results require validated instruments and clinical interpretation.

\medterm{persuasive communication} Communication intended to influence beliefs, decisions, or behavior through information, reasoning, emotion, or framing; healthcare use should preserve informed choice and avoid coercion.

\medterm{pertussis toxin} pertussis toxin describes an infectious or toxic process involving the site or exposure named by the term. Diagnosis considers the cause, route of exposure, affected tissues, severity, and host response.

\medterm{pertussis vaccine} pertussis vaccine is a procedure, device, treatment, or care intervention; indications, benefits, risks, and follow-up depend on the patient and clinical goal.

\medterm{pertuzumab} pertuzumab is a medication or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{pestalotiopsis} pestalotiopsis is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{pesticide} pesticide is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{petechia} A petechia is a tiny nonblanching red, purple, or brown spot caused by bleeding from small vessels into the skin or mucosa; numerous or rapidly spreading petechiae need assessment.

\medterm{petri dish} A Petri dish is a shallow lidded laboratory container used to culture microorganisms or cells and to hold specimens or small samples.

\medterm{petrolatum} petrolatum is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{pexophagy} Pexophagy is selective autophagic degradation of peroxisomes, allowing cells to remove damaged or excess organelles and recycle their components.

\medterm{P-glycoprotein} An ATP-dependent membrane transporter encoded by ABCB1 that pumps many drugs and other compounds out of cells, affecting intestinal absorption, tissue penetration, and drug interactions.

\medterm{pH meter} pH meter is a diagnostic test, specimen, imaging method, score, instrument, or measurement used to answer a clinical or laboratory question; results must be interpreted with the method and reference range.

\medterm{phagocyte} A phagocyte is an immune cell, such as a neutrophil or macrophage, that engulfs and digests microbes, dead cells, and foreign material.

\medterm{phagocytic vesicle} A phagocytic vesicle, or phagosome, is the membrane-bound compartment formed when a cell engulfs a particle before fusion with lysosomes for digestion.

\medterm{phagolysosome} A phagolysosome forms when a phagosome fuses with a lysosome, exposing engulfed microbes or debris to enzymes and toxic substances for destruction.

\medterm{phagophores} Phagophores are isolation membranes that expand around cytoplasmic material during autophagy and close to form autophagosomes.

\medterm{phalanx unspecified} A phalanx is one of the bones of a finger or toe; “unspecified” indicates that the particular digit or proximal, middle, or distal segment is not identified.

\medterm{phantom pain} phantom pain describes a clinical disorder or finding in the body system or process named by the term. Evaluation identifies the underlying mechanism, affected site, severity, and clinically relevant complications.

\medterm{pharmaceutical adjuvant} A pharmaceutical adjuvant is an inactive or auxiliary ingredient added to improve a medicine’s stability, absorption, preservation, delivery, or therapeutic effect.

\medterm{pharmaceutical excipient} A pharmaceutical excipient is a non-active ingredient in a dosage form, such as a filler, binder, coating, solvent, preservative, or disintegrant.

\medterm{pharmaceutical solutions} Liquid preparations in which one or more active pharmaceutical ingredients are dissolved in a suitable vehicle for administration, with concentration, sterility, stability, and route determining safe use.

\medterm{pharmacist} A pharmacist is a medication specialist who prepares, dispenses, reviews, and counsels about medicines and helps monitor interactions, dosing, safety, and adherence.

\medterm{pharmacogenomic testing} Testing that examines genetic variants affecting drug metabolism, transport, targets, or adverse reactions to help select or dose certain medicines.

\medterm{pharmacologic substance} A pharmacologic substance is a chemical that produces a biological effect by interacting with cells, receptors, enzymes, transporters, or other targets.

\medterm{pharmacotherapy} pharmacotherapy is used in relation to pharmacotherapy during assessment, diagnosis, treatment, monitoring, or patient support.

\medterm{pharmacy facility} A pharmacy facility is an authorized location where medicines may be stored, prepared, dispensed, monitored, and supplied under applicable professional and safety standards.

\medterm{pharyngeal carcinoma} pharyngeal carcinoma describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{pharyngeal disease} pharyngeal disease describes a clinical disorder or finding in the body system or process named by the term. Evaluation identifies the underlying mechanism, affected site, severity, and clinically relevant complications.

\medterm{pharyngeal hemorrhage} pharyngeal hemorrhage describes a clinical disorder or finding in the body system or process named by the term. Evaluation identifies the underlying mechanism, affected site, severity, and clinically relevant complications.

\medterm{pharyngeal mucositis} Inflammation and ulceration of the throat lining, commonly caused by radiation, chemotherapy, infection, trauma, or medications and producing pain, difficulty swallowing, and reduced intake.

\medterm{pharyngeal stenosis} pharyngeal stenosis describes a clinical disorder or finding in the body system or process named by the term. Evaluation identifies the underlying mechanism, affected site, severity, and clinically relevant complications.

\medterm{pharyngectomy} pharyngectomy is used in relation to pharyngectomy during assessment, diagnosis, treatment, monitoring, or patient support.

\medterm{pharyngolaryngeal pain} pharyngolaryngeal pain describes a clinical disorder or finding in the body system or process named by the term. Evaluation identifies the underlying mechanism, affected site, severity, and clinically relevant complications.

\medterm{pharynx necrosis} Death of tissue in the throat caused by severe infection, ischemia, radiation, caustic injury, or tumor, with risk of deep infection, bleeding, airway compromise, and sepsis.

\medterm{pharynx neoplasm} pharynx neoplasm describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{phase-contrast microscopy} phase-contrast microscopy is a diagnostic test, specimen, imaging method, score, instrument, or measurement used to answer a clinical or laboratory question; results must be interpreted with the method and reference range.

\medterm{phenacetin} Phenacetin is a former analgesic and fever-reducing drug withdrawn or restricted because long-term use can cause kidney damage and increase cancer risk.

\medterm{phenazopyridine} Phenazopyridine is a urinary analgesic that temporarily relieves dysuria without treating its cause; it can turn urine orange and prolonged or excessive use may cause haemolysis or methemoglobinaemia.

\medterm{phenelzine} Phenelzine is an irreversible monoamine-oxidase inhibitor antidepressant used selectively for treatment-resistant depression; dietary restrictions and drug interactions are important.

\medterm{phenethyl isothiocyanate} Phenethyl isothiocyanate is a sulfur-containing compound found in some plants and studied experimentally for antimicrobial and anticancer effects.

\medterm{phenethylamines} Phenethylamines are a chemical family including neurotransmitters, medicines, and psychoactive substances that can affect adrenergic, dopaminergic, or serotonergic signaling.

\medterm{phenformin} Phenformin is a former biguanide diabetes medicine withdrawn because it can cause life-threatening lactic acidosis.

\medterm{phenobarbital} Phenobarbital is a long-acting barbiturate antiseizure medicine that enhances GABA-A receptor inhibition; sedation, respiratory depression, cognitive effects, dependence, and enzyme-inducing drug interactions are important risks.

\medterm{phenocopy} A phenocopy is a trait resembling an inherited phenotype but produced by an environmental exposure, disease, or other non-genetic cause.

\medterm{phenol} phenol is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{phenolphthalein} phenolphthalein is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{phenols} phenols is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{phenothiazine} Phenothiazine is a chemical class that includes several antipsychotic and antiemetic medicines, with possible sedation, movement, and cardiac adverse effects.

\medterm{phenothiazines} Phenothiazines are medicines that chiefly block dopamine receptors and are used for selected psychotic, behavioral, or nausea-related conditions.

\medterm{phenoxodiol} Phenoxodiol is an investigational anticancer compound studied for effects on apoptosis and cellular signaling.

\medterm{phenoxybenzamine} Phenoxybenzamine is an irreversible alpha-adrenergic blocker used mainly before surgery for catecholamine-secreting tumors and sometimes for urinary symptoms.

\medterm{phenprocoumon} Phenprocoumon is a long-acting vitamin-K antagonist anticoagulant that reduces clotting-factor production and requires coagulation monitoring.

\medterm{phentermine} Phentermine is a sympathomimetic appetite suppressant used short term in selected weight-management plans; cardiovascular and dependence risks require clinical supervision.

\medterm{phenthoate} Phenthoate is an organophosphate insecticide that can inhibit acetylcholinesterase and cause cholinergic poisoning after exposure.

\medterm{phentolamine} Phentolamine is a short-acting alpha-adrenergic blocker used for catecholamine-related hypertension and to limit tissue injury after certain vasoconstrictor leaks.

\medterm{phenylacetate} phenylacetate is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{phenylalanine} phenylalanine is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{phenylbutyrate} phenylbutyrate is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{phenylephrine} phenylephrine is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{phenylpropanolamine} phenylpropanolamine is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{phenytoin} Phenytoin is an antiseizure medicine that blocks voltage-gated sodium channels and treats focal and tonic-clonic seizures; nystagmus, ataxia, gingival hyperplasia, rash, bone effects, and dose-related toxicity may occur.

\medterm{phenytoin sodium} phenytoin sodium is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{pheromone} A pheromone is a chemical signal released by an organism that alters behavior or physiology in another member of the same species.

\medterm{phialemonium atrogriseum} phialemonium atrogriseum is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{phialophora} phialophora is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{phialophora verrucosa} phialophora verrucosa is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{phlebovirus} phlebovirus is a virus or viral group classified by its genetic and biologic features. Clinical importance depends on the strain, host, route of transmission, tissue tropism, and ability to cause disease.

\medterm{phlegm} Phlegm is thick mucus produced in the lower airways and expelled by coughing; its amount, color, blood, and accompanying symptoms can help assess respiratory disease.

\medterm{phobia} A persistent excessive fear of a specific object, situation, or activity that causes marked distress or avoidance and is disproportionate to the actual danger.

\medterm{phonation} Production of voiced sound when exhaled air makes the vocal folds vibrate in the larynx; disorders cause hoarseness, weak voice, or loss of voice.

\medterm{phorate} Phorate is a highly toxic organophosphate pesticide whose exposure can produce excessive cholinergic activity and medical emergency.

\medterm{phorbol ester} A phorbol ester is a plant-derived compound that activates protein kinase C and is used in research on inflammation, signaling, and tumor promotion.

\medterm{phorbols} Phorbols are diterpene compounds related to phorbol esters that can activate cellular protein kinase C signaling.

\medterm{phosgene} A highly toxic industrial gas that reacts with moist respiratory tissues and can cause delayed pulmonary edema, cough, chest tightness, and respiratory failure after exposure.

\medterm{phosgene oxime} A highly corrosive chemical warfare agent that causes immediate severe pain, skin and eye injury, airway damage, and tissue necrosis on contact.

\medterm{phosmet} Phosmet is an organophosphate insecticide that can inhibit acetylcholinesterase and cause cholinergic toxicity after substantial exposure.

\medterm{phosphamidon} phosphamidon is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{phosphatidic acid} A membrane phospholipid and signaling intermediate that serves as a precursor for other glycerolipids and influences membrane curvature, trafficking, and cell growth.

\medterm{phosphatidylcholine} phosphatidylcholine is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{phosphatidylethanolamines} phosphatidylethanolamines is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{phosphatidylinositol} phosphatidylinositol is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{phosphatidylserine} phosphatidylserine is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{phosphatidylserines} phosphatidylserines is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{phosphine} phosphine is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{pigmentation disorder} pigmentation disorder describes a clinical disorder or finding in the body system or process named by the term. Evaluation identifies the underlying mechanism, affected site, severity, and clinically relevant complications.

\medterm{pilocarpine} Pilocarpine is a muscarinic cholinergic agonist that increases glandular secretion and causes pupillary constriction; it treats dry mouth or eyes and, topically, glaucoma, but can cause sweating, bronchospasm, and bradycardia.

\medterm{pilocytic astrocytoma} Pilocytic astrocytoma is usually a slow-growing low-grade astrocytic brain tumor, often occurring in children and commonly involving the cerebellum or optic pathway.

\medterm{piloerection} Elevation of hair shafts producing gooseflesh, caused by contraction of arrector pili muscles under sympathetic stimulation from cold, fear, pain, fever, or certain drugs.

\medterm{pilomatrixoma} Pilomatrixoma is a usually benign skin tumor derived from hair-matrix cells, presenting as a firm subcutaneous nodule, often on the head or neck.

\medterm{pilonidal fistula} An abnormal tunnel from a pilonidal sinus to the skin, usually in the natal cleft, that can drain pus or recur after abscess formation.

\medterm{pilot project} A pilot project is a small preliminary implementation used to test feasibility, identify problems, and refine methods before broader rollout.

\medterm{pilus} A pilus is a hair-like bacterial surface structure used for attachment, movement, or transfer of genetic material between cells.

\medterm{pimozide} Pimozide is a first-generation antipsychotic used selectively for tic disorders; movement disorders, QT prolongation, and drug interactions require monitoring.

\medterm{pinch strength} Pinch strength is the force produced when the thumb presses against one or more fingers and can help assess hand, nerve, muscle, and functional impairment.

\medterm{pindolol} pindolol is a medication or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{pineal cyst} A pineal cyst is a fluid-filled lesion of the pineal gland, usually incidental and harmless but occasionally associated with headache, hydrocephalus, or visual symptoms.

\medterm{pineal germinoma} A pineal germinoma is a malignant germ-cell tumor in the pineal region, often causing headache, eye-movement abnormalities, or hydrocephalus and usually responsive to radiotherapy and chemotherapy.

\medterm{pineoblastoma} pineoblastoma describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{pineocytoma} Pineocytoma is a usually slow-growing tumor of pineal cells that may obstruct cerebrospinal-fluid flow and cause raised intracranial pressure.

\medterm{pinhole meatus} A pinhole meatus is an abnormally narrow external opening, commonly referring to the urethral meatus, that can obstruct urine flow or complicate catheterization.

\medterm{pinocytosis} Cellular uptake of extracellular fluid and dissolved molecules through small membrane vesicles, allowing cells to sample their environment and internalize nutrients or signaling substances.

\medterm{pint} A pint is a unit of volume; its exact amount varies by measurement system, so the local standard must be specified when measuring fluids or medicines.

\medterm{pinta} Pinta is a chronic skin infection caused by Treponema carateum, producing changing stages of pigmented or depigmented lesions and treated with antibiotics.

\medterm{pioglitazone} Pioglitazone is a PPAR-gamma agonist that improves insulin sensitivity in type 2 diabetes; weight gain, oedema, heart-failure worsening, fractures, and bladder-cancer concerns require patient-specific assessment.

\medterm{pipemidic acid} An older quinolone antibacterial formerly used mainly for urinary-tract infections; resistance and safety limitations have greatly reduced its clinical use.

\medterm{pipendoxifene} Pipendoxifene is an investigational selective estrogen-receptor modulator studied for possible effects on breast, bone, and reproductive tissues.

\medterm{piperacillin} Piperacillin is an extended-spectrum penicillin antibiotic often combined with tazobactam to treat serious infections including Pseudomonas; allergy, diarrhoea, cytopenias, electrolyte effects, and renal dosing are important.

\medterm{piperazine} piperazine is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{piperonyl butoxide} piperonyl butoxide describes an infectious or toxic process involving the site or exposure named by the term. Diagnosis considers the cause, route of exposure, affected tissues, severity, and host response.

\medterm{pipette} A pipette is a laboratory instrument used to measure and transfer small volumes of liquid accurately.

\medterm{pipobroman} Pipobroman is an oral alkylating antineoplastic drug used historically for some blood disorders and associated with marrow suppression and later cancer risk.

\medterm{pirfenidone} Pirfenidone is an antifibrotic medicine used to slow decline in idiopathic pulmonary fibrosis; nausea, photosensitivity, fatigue, and liver-enzyme elevation may occur and require monitoring.

\medterm{pirimicarb} Pirimicarb is a selective carbamate insecticide targeting aphids; exposure can inhibit acetylcholinesterase and cause cholinergic symptoms.

\medterm{pirinitramide} Pirinitramide is a synthetic opioid analgesic used historically in some countries; opioid effects include sedation, constipation, dependence, and respiratory depression.

\medterm{piritrexim} Piritrexim is an investigational antifolate that inhibits dihydrofolate reductase and has been studied as an anticancer agent.

\medterm{piromidic acid} An older quinolone-like antibacterial investigated or used for urinary and gastrointestinal infections; current use depends on local availability, susceptibility, and safety guidance.

\medterm{piroxicam} piroxicam is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{pisiform bone} A small pea-shaped carpal bone embedded in the tendon of flexor carpi ulnaris on the ulnar side of the wrist; fracture or arthritis can cause localized wrist pain.

\medterm{pithomyces} pithomyces is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{pituicytoma} Pituicytoma is a rare typically slow-growing tumor arising from specialized glial cells of the pituitary stalk or posterior pituitary region.

\medterm{pituitary} The pituitary is an endocrine gland at the base of the brain that releases hormones controlling growth, thyroid and adrenal function, reproduction, lactation, and water balance.

\medterm{pituitary carcinoma} pituitary carcinoma describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{pituitary disease} pituitary disease describes a clinical disorder or finding in the body system or process named by the term. Evaluation identifies the underlying mechanism, affected site, severity, and clinically relevant complications.

\medterm{pituitary dwarfism} Pituitary dwarfism is severe short stature caused by deficient growth hormone or broader pituitary dysfunction, with evaluation focused on endocrine and genetic causes.

\medterm{pituitary hormone} A hormone secreted by the anterior or posterior pituitary that regulates growth, thyroid and adrenal function, reproduction, lactation, water balance, or other endocrine organs.

\medterm{pituitary neoplasm} pituitary neoplasm describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{pityriasis lichenoides} An uncommon inflammatory skin disorder producing recurrent small scaly papules, with forms ranging from mild chronic lesions to an acute ulceronecrotic eruption.

\medterm{pivampicillin} pivampicillin is a medication or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{pixantrone} Pixantrone is an anthracenedione antineoplastic drug developed for selected aggressive lymphomas, with potential marrow, infection, and cardiac risks.

\medterm{place} Place describes a location or position; in healthcare, the relevant place may be an anatomic site, care setting, or environmental context.

\medterm{placebo control} A placebo control is an inactive treatment designed to resemble the intervention in a clinical trial, helping separate treatment effects from expectation and natural change.

\medterm{placenta accreta} Placenta accreta spectrum occurs when placental tissue abnormally attaches to or invades the uterine wall, creating major risk of hemorrhage during attempted delivery or removal.

\medterm{placenta disorder} placenta disorder describes a clinical disorder or finding in the body system or process named by the term. Evaluation identifies the underlying mechanism, affected site, severity, and clinically relevant complications.

\medterm{placenta percreta} Placenta percreta is the most invasive form of placenta accreta spectrum, with villi growing through the uterine wall and sometimes into nearby organs such as the bladder.

\medterm{placental hormone} A hormone produced by placental tissue, such as hCG, progesterone, estrogens, or human placental lactogen, that supports pregnancy, fetal growth, and maternal metabolic adaptation.

\medterm{placental infarction} A region of placental tissue damaged by inadequate maternal blood flow and coagulation, which may be incidental or, when extensive, associated with fetal growth restriction or pregnancy complications.

\medterm{placental lactogen} Placental lactogen is a hormone made by the placenta that alters maternal metabolism and supports breast development and fetal nutrient availability during pregnancy.

\medterm{placentation} The developmental process by which the placenta forms and attaches to the uterus, establishes maternal--fetal circulation, and develops the exchange and endocrine functions needed for pregnancy.

\medterm{plagiocephaly} Plagiocephaly is asymmetric flattening or shaping of an infant’s skull, caused by position or premature suture fusion; its evaluation distinguishes benign molding from craniosynostosis.

\medterm{planococcaceae} Planococcaceae is a family of Gram-positive bacteria found in environmental habitats; clinical importance depends on the species and site of recovery.

\medterm{planococcus bacteria} planococcus bacteria is a microorganism or microbial group identified by its biologic and taxonomic features. Clinical significance depends on pathogenicity, exposure, the site involved, and antimicrobial susceptibility.

\medterm{plant development} Plant development is the progression from seed or meristem through growth, differentiation, flowering, reproduction, and senescence.

\medterm{plant extract} A plant extract is a preparation made by removing selected constituents from plant material; composition, dose, contamination, interactions, and evidence vary widely.

\medterm{plant oils} Plant oils are lipid mixtures extracted from seeds, fruits, or other plant parts and used as foods, medicines, cosmetics, or industrial materials.

\medterm{plant part} A plant part is a structural component such as a root, stem, leaf, flower, fruit, or seed that may contain distinct nutrients, chemicals, or medicinal constituents.

\medterm{plant poisoning} plant poisoning describes an infectious or toxic process involving the site or exposure named by the term. Diagnosis considers the cause, route of exposure, affected tissues, severity, and host response.

\medterm{plant proteins} Proteins supplied by legumes, grains, nuts, seeds, vegetables, and other plants; combining varied sources can provide essential amino acids, with nutritional needs depending on age and health.

\medterm{plant tumor} plant tumor describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{plant virus} plant virus is a virus or viral group classified by its genetic and biologic features. Clinical importance depends on the strain, host, route of transmission, tissue tropism, and ability to cause disease.

\medterm{plantar nerve} A nerve supplying sensation to the sole of the foot and motor branches to intrinsic foot muscles; compression can cause burning pain, numbness, or altered toe movement.

\medterm{plantar wart} An anatomical structure, tissue, or physiologic process with the location or function indicated by the term; disease or injury changes its expected findings.

\medterm{plantaris muscle} A small posterior-leg muscle with a long tendon that assists weakly with knee flexion and plantar flexion; it can be strained during sudden athletic movement.

\medterm{plasma cell neoplasm} A clonal disorder of antibody-producing plasma cells, ranging from localized plasmacytoma to multiple myeloma with marrow, bone, kidney, calcium, or blood abnormalities.

\medterm{plasma kallikrein} plasma kallikrein is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{plasma membrane} The selectively permeable lipid bilayer surrounding a cell, containing proteins that control transport, signaling, adhesion, recognition, and communication with the extracellular environment.

\medterm{plasma substitutes} plasma substitutes is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{plasmablastic lymphoma} plasmablastic lymphoma describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{plasmalogen} plasmalogen is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{plasmapheresis} Process of separating and removing plasma from blood, replacing it with donor plasma or albumin; used to treat autoimmune conditions and remove pathogenic antibodies. Its significance is established from its characteristic features, causes, and clinical context.

\medterm{plasmid} A plasmid is a small usually circular DNA molecule separate from a bacterial chromosome that can replicate independently and sometimes carries antibiotic-resistance genes.

\medterm{plasminogen} Plasminogen is the inactive circulating precursor of plasmin, the enzyme that degrades fibrin; tissue or urokinase-type activators convert it to plasmin during fibrinolysis.

\medterm{plasminogen activator} A plasminogen activator is an enzyme, such as tissue plasminogen activator or urokinase, that converts plasminogen to plasmin and promotes fibrin clot breakdown; therapeutic use carries bleeding risk.

\medterm{plasminogen inactivators} plasminogen inactivators is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.
