\medterm{plasmodesma} A plasmodesma is a microscopic channel through a plant cell wall connecting neighboring cells and allowing controlled movement of molecules and signals.

\medterm{plasmodium} plasmodium is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{pleural neoplasm} pleural neoplasm describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{pleural sac} An anatomical structure, tissue, or physiologic process with the location or function indicated by the term; disease or injury changes its expected findings.

\medterm{pleural tuberculosis} Tuberculous infection or inflammation of the pleura, often producing a lymphocyte-rich pleural effusion, chest pain, fever, and breathlessness and requiring microbiologic or tissue evaluation.

\medterm{pleuritic pain} pleuritic pain describes a clinical disorder or finding in the body system or process named by the term. Evaluation identifies the underlying mechanism, affected site, severity, and clinically relevant complications.

\medterm{pleuropulmonary blastoma} pleuropulmonary blastoma describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{plexiform neurofibroma} A plexiform neurofibroma is a diffuse nerve-sheath tumor, strongly associated with neurofibromatosis type 1, that can grow along multiple nerve branches and sometimes become malignant.

\medterm{pliability} Pliability is the ability of a material or tissue to bend, stretch, or deform without breaking; it is relevant to skin, vessels, lungs, and medical devices.

\medterm{plitidepsin} Plitidepsin is an antineoplastic compound that targets eukaryotic elongation factor 1A and has been studied for selected cancers and viral infections.

\medterm{Ploidies} Ploidy describes the number of complete chromosome sets in a cell, such as haploid, diploid, or polyploid; abnormal ploidy can characterize tumors or developmental disorders.

\medterm{plozalizumab} plozalizumab is a medication or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{pluralibacter gergoviae} pluralibacter gergoviae describes an infectious or toxic process involving the site or exposure named by the term. Diagnosis considers the cause, route of exposure, affected tissues, severity, and host response.

\medterm{pluripotent stem cell} A stem cell capable of producing derivatives of all three embryonic germ layers but not, by itself, a complete organism; embryonic and induced pluripotent stem cells are major research sources.

\medterm{plutonium} Plutonium is a radioactive heavy metal whose inhalation, ingestion, or embedded contamination can cause radiation injury and long-term cancer risk.

\medterm{pneumococcal infection} pneumococcal infection describes an infectious or toxic process involving the site or exposure named by the term. Diagnosis considers the cause, route of exposure, affected tissues, severity, and host response.

\medterm{pneumococcal vaccine} pneumococcal vaccine is a procedure, device, treatment, or care intervention; indications, benefits, risks, and follow-up depend on the patient and clinical goal.

\medterm{pneumocystis} pneumocystis is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{pneumocystis carinii} pneumocystis carinii is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{pneumocystis infection} pneumocystis infection describes an infectious or toxic process involving the site or exposure named by the term. Diagnosis considers the cause, route of exposure, affected tissues, severity, and host response.

\medterm{pneumocystis jiroveci} Pneumocystis jirovecii is a fungus that causes opportunistic pneumonia, especially in advanced HIV or other cellular immunodeficiency; symptoms include progressive dry cough, fever, and hypoxaemia, and diagnosis requires appropriate respiratory testing.

\medterm{pneumovirinae} pneumovirinae is a medication or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{pneumovirus} pneumovirus is a virus or viral group classified by its genetic and biologic features. Clinical importance depends on the strain, host, route of transmission, tissue tropism, and ability to cause disease.

\medterm{pocket erosion} Loss of tissue at the margin or base of a periodontal or surgical pocket, potentially exposing deeper structures and promoting infection, bleeding, or impaired healing.

\medterm{podiatrist} A podiatrist is a clinician specializing in assessment and treatment of the feet, ankles, and related lower-limb problems within the scope of local practice.

\medterm{podocyte} A podocyte is a specialized epithelial cell covering glomerular capillaries; injury to its filtration slits contributes to proteinuria and kidney disease.

\medterm{podofilox} Podofilox is a topical antimitotic medicine used to treat selected external anogenital warts caused by human papillomavirus; irritation and correct application are important.

\medterm{podophyllin} Podophyllin is a plant-derived resin with antimitotic activity formerly used topically for warts; it can be severely toxic if absorbed and is generally replaced by safer products.

\medterm{podosome} A podosome is an actin-rich adhesion and matrix-degrading structure in cells such as macrophages and osteoclasts.

\medterm{poikilocytosis} Marked variation in the shape of red blood cells on a blood smear, seen in iron deficiency, thalassemia, marrow disease, hemolysis, and other anemias.

\medterm{poikiloderma} A skin pattern combining mottled pigmentation, visible small vessels, and epidermal thinning, seen in connective-tissue disease, chronic sun damage, inflammation, and some cutaneous lymphomas.

\medterm{pokeweed mitogen} Pokeweed mitogen is a plant lectin used experimentally to stimulate lymphocyte proliferation and investigate immune-cell responses.

\medterm{pol gene} The HIV gene encoding enzymes required for viral replication, including protease, reverse transcriptase, and integrase; antiretroviral drugs target these products.

\medterm{polarity} Polarity is directional organization or unequal distribution of charge, structure, or function in a cell, molecule, tissue, or physical system.

\medterm{polarography} Polarography is an electrochemical analytical method that measures current while voltage changes at an electrode to identify or quantify reducible substances.

\medterm{polidocanol} Polidocanol is a detergent sclerosant injected to close selected varicose veins or vascular lesions by damaging the vessel lining and promoting fibrosis.

\medterm{poliosis} A localized patch of white or depigmented hair caused by reduced melanin in follicles, occurring in vitiligo, alopecia areata, inflammatory disease, genetic syndromes, or after injury.

\medterm{poliovirus vaccine} poliovirus vaccine is a virus or viral group classified by its genetic and biologic features. Clinical importance depends on the strain, host, route of transmission, tissue tropism, and ability to cause disease.

\medterm{pollakisuria} Pollakisuria is unusually frequent urination, often in small amounts, caused by irritation, infection, stones, bladder disorders, anxiety, or other urinary conditions.

\medterm{pollen} Pollen is the fine reproductive powder released by plants; airborne pollen commonly triggers seasonal allergic rhinitis, conjunctivitis, or asthma.

\medterm{pollen tube} A pollen tube is a tube grown by a germinating pollen grain that carries sperm cells through plant tissue to the ovule for fertilization.

\medterm{pollination} Transfer of pollen from anthers to a receptive stigma or ovule, allowing fertilization in flowering plants; it is not a human reproductive process.

\medterm{poly A} poly A is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{poly I-C} poly I-C is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{poly ICLC} poly ICLC is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{polyadenylation} Addition of a polyadenosine tail to the 3′ end of many eukaryotic messenger RNAs, promoting stability, nuclear export, and efficient translation.

\medterm{polyamine} polyamine is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{polyamine catabolism} polyamine catabolism is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{polyarthritis} Inflammation or disease affecting five or more joints, caused by autoimmune arthritis, infection, crystal disease, connective-tissue disease, or other systemic conditions.

\medterm{polybrominated biphenyl} polybrominated biphenyl is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{polychlorinated biphenyl} polychlorinated biphenyl is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{polyene} polyene is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{polypropylene} polypropylene is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{polyradiculopathy} Disease affecting multiple spinal nerve roots, producing pain, weakness, sensory loss, or reflex changes in a distribution determined by the involved roots.

\medterm{Pontiac fever} Pontiac fever describes an infectious or toxic process involving the site or exposure named by the term. Diagnosis considers the cause, route of exposure, affected tissues, severity, and host response.

\medterm{popliteal cyst} A fluid-filled swelling behind the knee, also called a Baker cyst, caused by communication between the joint and a bursa and often associated with arthritis, meniscal injury, or inflammation.

\medterm{population density} The number of people living in a defined area, a demographic measure relevant to crowding, resource needs, environmental exposure, and spread of communicable disease.

\medterm{population genetics} The study of how gene frequencies and genetic variation change within and between populations through inheritance, mutation, selection, migration, and genetic drift.

\medterm{population group} A defined set of people sharing a characteristic such as age, location, exposure, ancestry, occupation, or health condition for epidemiologic or clinical analysis.

\medterm{population health} The health outcomes of a defined group and the distribution of those outcomes, considered in relation to social conditions, environment, behavior, healthcare, and policy.

\medterm{pore} A pore is a small opening or channel in a surface, membrane, tissue, or material that permits passage of fluid, gas, particles, or cellular contents.

\medterm{porokeratosis} A group of keratinization disorders producing characteristic ridge-like borders called cornoid lamellae around annular scaly lesions, with some forms carrying a small skin-cancer risk.

\medterm{poroma} A poroma is a usually benign sweat-gland tumor presenting as a flesh-colored or reddish papule or nodule, often on palms or soles.

\medterm{porphyria} A group of disorders of heme synthesis that cause accumulation of porphyrin precursors and may produce acute neurologic or abdominal attacks, photosensitivity, or both.

\medterm{porphyrin} A porphyrin is a ring-shaped molecule that binds metals in compounds such as heme; abnormal porphyrin metabolism causes the porphyrias.

\medterm{portion of cytosol} A region of the cell’s aqueous internal fluid outside membrane-bound organelles, containing ions, metabolites, proteins, and enzymes that support cellular reactions.

\medterm{port-wine stain} A congenital capillary malformation causing a persistent pink, red, or purple flat patch of skin; facial lesions may be associated with Sturge--Weber syndrome.

\medterm{positive} Positive means that a test, finding, result, or response is present or above a defined threshold; interpretation depends on the test and clinical setting.

\medterm{positive finding} A positive finding is an observed symptom, examination sign, image, laboratory result, or test response that supports the presence of a specified condition or feature.

\medterm{positron camera} A positron camera detects paired gamma rays from positron annihilation and is used in positron-emission tomography to map tracer distribution.

\medterm{postal service} A postal service transports letters, packages, specimens, or medicines; healthcare shipping requires appropriate packaging, labeling, temperature control, and privacy.

\medterm{posterior rhinorrhea} Posterior rhinorrhea is mucus draining from the nose into the throat, commonly causing throat clearing or cough from allergy, infection, irritation, or sinus disease.

\medterm{postganglionic fiber} An autonomic nerve fiber extending from a ganglion to its target organ after the synapse, carrying sympathetic or parasympathetic signals to smooth muscle, glands, or the heart.

\medterm{postnatal care} postnatal care is a medical procedure, treatment, device, or care topic; indications, benefits, risks, and follow-up depend on the patient and clinical goal.

\medterm{postoperative hemorrhage} postoperative hemorrhage describes a clinical disorder or finding in the body system or process named by the term. Evaluation identifies the underlying mechanism, affected site, severity, and clinically relevant complications.

\medterm{postoperative infection} postoperative infection describes an infectious or toxic process involving the site or exposure named by the term. Diagnosis considers the cause, route of exposure, affected tissues, severity, and host response.

\medterm{postoperative period} The postoperative period is the time after an operation when recovery, pain control, wound care, mobility, nutrition, complications, and follow-up are monitored.

\medterm{postprandial period} The postprandial period is the interval after eating when digestion and nutrient absorption occur and glucose, insulin, and other metabolic responses change.

\medterm{postsynaptic membrane} The specialized receiving surface of a neuron or muscle cell at a synapse, containing receptors and signaling proteins that respond to neurotransmitter released by the presynaptic cell.

\medterm{potassium channel} potassium channel is a medical term describing the named concept; its precise interpretation depends on its defining features, associated findings, and clinical context.

\medterm{potassium iodide} potassium iodide is a medicine or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dosing, contraindications, interactions, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{potassium ion} The positively charged form of potassium, the principal intracellular cation that helps establish resting membrane potential, nerve transmission, muscle contraction, and cardiac rhythm.

\medterm{potential} Potential describes a capacity, possibility, or stored energy that may become clinically relevant depending on circumstances and additional findings.

\medterm{potentiometry} potentiometry is a laboratory test, imaging method, instrument, score, or measurement used to characterize a specimen or clinical finding; interpretation depends on the method and clinical context.

\medterm{poultice} A poultice is a moist or semi-solid preparation applied to the skin, historically to soothe or draw fluid; evidence and safety depend on its ingredients and use.

\medterm{pound} A pound is a unit of mass or force depending on context; its exact conversion should be specified when calculating body weight or medication dosing.

\medterm{povidone-iodine} povidone-iodine is a medicine or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dosing, contraindications, interactions, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{powder diffraction} An analytical method that identifies crystalline substances by measuring how an X-ray or other beam is diffracted by powdered material.

\medterm{poxviridae infection} poxviridae infection identifies poxviridae infection, a biological taxon or infectious-disease concept whose clinical importance depends on the organism, host, and site involved.

\medterm{ppar alpha} PPAR-alpha is a nuclear receptor that regulates fatty-acid oxidation and lipid metabolism and is activated by fibrate medicines.

\medterm{ppar delta} PPAR-delta is a nuclear receptor involved in fatty-acid use, energy balance, inflammation, and tissue differentiation.

\medterm{pracinostat} Pracinostat is an investigational histone-deacetylase inhibitor studied in myeloid and other cancers by altering gene expression and cell survival.

\medterm{practitioner} A person trained and authorized to provide a professional service, especially diagnosis, treatment, or healthcare, within a defined scope of practice.

\medterm{pragmatic trial} A pragmatic trial evaluates an intervention under usual real-world care conditions to estimate how well it works in routine practice.

\medterm{pralatrexate} pralatrexate is a medicine or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dosing, contraindications, interactions, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{pravastatin} pravastatin is a medication or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{pravastatin sodium} pravastatin sodium is a medicine or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{praziquantel} Praziquantel is an antiparasitic drug that disrupts parasite calcium handling and is used for schistosomiasis and several tapeworm or fluke infections.

\medterm{prealbumin} Prealbumin, or transthyretin, is a liver-produced transport protein for thyroid hormone and retinol-binding protein; its level is influenced by inflammation and nutrition.

\medterm{precancerous condition} precancerous condition describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{precancerous polyp} precancerous polyp describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{precipitating factor} A precipitating factor is an event or exposure that triggers the onset or worsening of symptoms or disease in a susceptible person.

\medterm{preclinical study} preclinical study is a developmental, genetic, reproductive, or family-related term describing a stage, trait, relationship, or pregnancy history; its meaning depends on the clinical context.

\medterm{preconception care} preconception care is a medical procedure, treatment, device, or care topic; indications, benefits, risks, and follow-up depend on the patient and clinical goal.

\medterm{prediabetes syndrome} prediabetes syndrome describes a clinical disorder or finding in the body system or process named by the term. Evaluation identifies the underlying mechanism, affected site, severity, and clinically relevant complications.

\medterm{prednimustine} Prednimustine is a former antineoplastic drug combining a corticosteroid-related group with an alkylating component and causing immunosuppression and marrow toxicity.

\medterm{prednisolone} prednisolone is a medicine or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dosing, contraindications, interactions, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{prednisone} prednisone is a medicine or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dosing, contraindications, interactions, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{preexcitation syndrome} preexcitation syndrome describes a clinical disorder or finding in the body system or process named by the term. Evaluation identifies the underlying mechanism, affected site, severity, and clinically relevant complications.

\medterm{prefix} A prefix is a word element placed before a root to modify meaning; medical prefixes commonly indicate location, number, time, direction, or abnormality.

\medterm{prefrontal cortex} The prefrontal cortex is the front part of the frontal lobe involved in planning, judgment, working memory, impulse control, and social behavior.

\medterm{pregabalin} pregabalin is a medicine or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dosing, contraindications, interactions, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{preganglionic fiber} An autonomic nerve fiber that runs from the central nervous system to an autonomic ganglion, where it synapses with a postganglionic neuron.

\medterm{pregnancy complication} A maternal, fetal, placental, or labor-related problem that disrupts an otherwise expected pregnancy, such as preeclampsia, hemorrhage, diabetes, growth restriction, or preterm labor.

\medterm{pregnancy outcome} Pregnancy outcome describes what happens to a pregnancy, such as live birth, miscarriage, stillbirth, preterm birth, or other maternal or fetal result.

\medterm{pregnancy status} Pregnancy status indicates whether a person is pregnant, not pregnant, or of unknown status and can affect medication, imaging, and treatment decisions.

\medterm{pregnancy trimester} A pregnancy trimester is one of three approximate stages of gestation used to organize fetal development, screening, risks, and clinical care.

\medterm{pregnane} Pregnane is a 21-carbon steroid hydrocarbon that forms the structural backbone of progesterone and several corticosteroid and progestin compounds.

\medterm{pregnanolone} Pregnanolone is a neuroactive steroid metabolite that can modulate GABA-A receptors and influence neuronal excitability, mood, and sedation.

\medterm{pregnenolone} Pregnenolone is a steroid hormone precursor made from cholesterol that can be converted into progesterone, corticosteroids, and sex hormones.

\medterm{premalignant} Premalignant describes abnormal tissue changes that are not yet invasive cancer but have an increased risk of progressing to malignancy.

\medterm{premature adrenarche} Early appearance of pubic or axillary hair, body odor, or mild acne from adrenal androgen production without true central puberty; evaluation excludes virilizing disorders.

\medterm{premature closure} Closure of a body opening, developmental structure, or growth plate earlier than expected, potentially causing obstruction, abnormal shape, or impaired growth depending on the site.

\medterm{premature labor} Regular uterine contractions with cervical change before 37 completed weeks of pregnancy, which can lead to preterm birth and requires assessment of membranes, infection, and fetal well-being.

\medterm{premature thelarche} Isolated breast development in a young child without other signs of puberty, often transient but assessed for growth acceleration, estrogen exposure, or true precocious puberty.

\medterm{pre-medication} Medicine given before a procedure, treatment, or exposure to prevent a predictable problem such as pain, nausea, allergy, infection, or anxiety.

\medterm{pre-miRNA} A pre-miRNA is an early hairpin-shaped microRNA transcript processed in the nucleus before becoming a mature regulator of gene expression.

\medterm{prenylation} Attachment of a lipid group to a protein, helping determine its membrane localization, signaling, stability, and interactions; abnormal prenylation contributes to some diseases and is a drug target.

\medterm{preoperative care} preoperative care is a medical procedure, treatment, device, or care topic; indications, benefits, risks, and follow-up depend on the patient and clinical goal.

\medterm{preoptic area} The preoptic area is a hypothalamic region involved in temperature regulation, sleep, reproductive behavior, and hormone control.

\medterm{presenile dementia} Presenile dementia is an older term for dementia beginning before the usual older-adult age range; modern diagnosis specifies the underlying disease and age at onset.

\medterm{presenilin} Presenilin is a component of the gamma-secretase complex that processes membrane proteins; inherited variants can cause familial early-onset Alzheimer disease.

\medterm{preservation} Actions that maintain the viability, structure, quality, or safety of tissue, cells, organs, specimens, food, or medicines during storage or transport.

\medterm{preservative} A preservative is a substance added to a medicine, food, or cosmetic to inhibit microbial growth or chemical deterioration and extend shelf life.

\medterm{pressoreceptor} A pressure-sensitive receptor, especially a baroreceptor in the carotid sinus or aortic arch, that detects arterial stretch and helps regulate heart rate and blood pressure.

\medterm{presynaptic terminals} Presynaptic terminals are the ends of neurons that release neurotransmitters into synapses in response to electrical activity.

\medterm{primary endpoint} A primary endpoint is the main prespecified outcome used to judge the effect of an intervention in a clinical trial.

\medterm{primary hypersomnia} Excessive daytime sleepiness not better explained by insufficient sleep, another sleep disorder, substance, or medical condition; modern classification uses specific central-hypersomnolence diagnoses.

\medterm{primary hypoparathyroidism} Inadequate parathyroid hormone production causing low calcium and high phosphate, with possible tingling, cramps, tetany, seizures, cataracts, or chronic bone and dental effects.

\medterm{primary hypothyroidism} Reduced thyroid-gland production of thyroxine and triiodothyronine, usually causing high TSH and symptoms such as fatigue, cold intolerance, constipation, dry skin, and slowed heart rate.

\medterm{primary microrna} A primary microRNA is the initial RNA transcript made from a microRNA gene before nuclear and cytoplasmic processing into mature microRNA.

\medterm{primidone} primidone is a medicine or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dosing, contraindications, interactions, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{primitive node} The primitive node is an embryonic organizer at the cranial end of the primitive streak that helps establish body axes and germ-layer formation.

\medterm{primitive streak} The primitive streak is an embryonic structure through which cells migrate during gastrulation to form the three germ layers and establish body organization.

\medterm{prinomastat} Prinomastat is an investigational matrix-metalloproteinase inhibitor studied for blocking enzymes involved in tumor invasion and tissue remodeling.

\medterm{privacy} Privacy is a person’s right and expectation that personal information, communications, and bodily matters are protected from unauthorized access or disclosure.

\medterm{privacy breach} A privacy breach is unauthorized access, use, disclosure, loss, or exposure of personal or health information.

\medterm{probability} Probability is a numerical expression of how likely an event is, ranging from impossible to certain and used in diagnosis, risk assessment, and research.

\medterm{probenecid} probenecid is a medicine or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dosing, contraindications, interactions, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{proboscis} A proboscis is an elongated mouthpart or snout used by some animals and insects to obtain food; certain insects can transmit infection through it.

\medterm{probucol} Probucol is a lipid-lowering drug that reduces cholesterol but may prolong the QT interval and is not widely used in current practice.

\medterm{procainamide} procainamide is a medicine or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dosing, contraindications, interactions, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{procaine} procaine is a medication or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{procarbazine} Procarbazine is an alkylating antineoplastic drug used in selected lymphoma and brain-tumor regimens; myelosuppression, nausea, and interactions are important.

\medterm{procarbazine hydrochloride} Procarbazine hydrochloride is the hydrochloride salt formulation of the antineoplastic drug procarbazine.

\medterm{procentriole} A procentriole is an immature centriole that duplicates beside a parent centriole and later matures to organize microtubules.

\medterm{procerus muscle} A small facial muscle over the bridge of the nose that draws the medial eyebrows downward and produces horizontal wrinkles between the eyebrows.

\medterm{processing bodies} Processing bodies are cytoplasmic RNA-protein granules where messenger RNAs may be stored, decapped, degraded, or regulated.

\medterm{processor} A processor is a device or biological system that performs a defined sequence of operations; in computing it executes instructions, while in medicine the meaning is context-dependent.

\medterm{prochlorperazine} prochlorperazine is a medicine or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dosing, contraindications, interactions, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{procollagen} procollagen is a biochemical, genetic, or chemical term used in medicine; its significance depends on its structure, function, concentration, pathway, or exposure.

\medterm{proctoscope} A proctoscope is a short instrument used to inspect the anal canal and lower rectum and may permit minor procedures or tissue sampling.

\medterm{proctoscopy} Proctoscopy is examination of the anal canal and rectum with a proctoscope to evaluate bleeding, pain, lesions, inflammation, or masses.

\medterm{product recall} Removal or correction of a medicine, device, food, or other product because it may be defective, contaminated, mislabeled, or unsafe for use.

\medterm{productive cough} A cough that brings up mucus or sputum from the airways, occurring with infection, chronic lung disease, bronchiectasis, asthma, aspiration, or other airway inflammation.

\medterm{proestrus} Proestrus is the phase of the reproductive cycle before estrus, characterized by follicular development and rising estrogen in many mammals.

\medterm{progesterone antagonist} progesterone antagonist is a medicine or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{progestin} A progestin is a synthetic progesterone-like hormone used in contraception, hormone therapy, and treatment of selected gynecologic conditions.

\medterm{prognostic factor} A prognostic factor is a clinical, pathologic, or biologic feature associated with the likely course, outcome, or survival of a disease.

\medterm{program development} Program development is the planned design, testing, implementation, and evaluation of a service, intervention, or software system.

\medterm{programmed instruction} A structured teaching method that presents small units of information with questions and immediate feedback, allowing learners to progress through material step by step.

\medterm{proinsulin} proinsulin is a medicine or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{projectile vomiting} Forceful vomiting expelled with unusual intensity, which may occur with gastric outlet obstruction, raised intracranial pressure, infection, migraine, or other causes; severity and associated signs guide evaluation.

\medterm{prokaryote} A prokaryote is a cell or organism without a membrane-bound nucleus, including bacteria and archaea.

\medterm{prolactin} Prolactin is a pituitary hormone that promotes breast development and milk production and also affects reproductive and immune functions.

\medterm{prolactin receptor} A cell-surface receptor activated by prolactin to regulate mammary development, milk production, reproduction, immune effects, and other tissue responses.

\medterm{prolapsed cord} A prolapsed umbilical cord descends beside or ahead of the fetus after membrane rupture, risking cord compression and reduced fetal oxygen; it is an obstetric emergency.

\medterm{proliferation} Increase in cell number through growth and division; controlled proliferation supports development and repair, whereas excessive or unregulated proliferation contributes to cancer and hyperplasia.

\medterm{prolindac} Prolindac is an investigational prodrug designed to release an active anticancer agent within tumors.

\medterm{proline} Proline is an amino acid with a cyclic structure that contributes to protein folding, collagen stability, and cellular metabolism.

\medterm{prolymphocyte count} The number or proportion of prolymphocytes, immature-appearing lymphoid cells, in blood or marrow; an increased count may occur in selected lymphoid leukemias and requires morphology and immunophenotyping.

\medterm{prolymphocytic leukemia} prolymphocytic leukemia describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{promazine} Promazine is a first-generation phenothiazine antipsychotic used historically for psychosis or severe agitation; sedation, low blood pressure, and movement effects can occur.

\medterm{promecarb} Promecarb is a carbamate insecticide that inhibits acetylcholinesterase and can cause cholinergic poisoning after substantial exposure.

\medterm{promegapoietin} Promegapoietin is an investigational hematopoietic growth-factor molecule designed to stimulate production of blood cells or platelets.

\medterm{prometaphase} Prometaphase is the stage of mitosis after nuclear-envelope breakdown when spindle microtubules attach to chromosome kinetochores.

\medterm{promethazine} promethazine is a medicine or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dosing, contraindications, interactions, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{promonocyte count} The number or proportion of promonocytes, immature monocyte precursors, in blood or marrow; increased cells may support evaluation of acute or chronic myeloid leukemia.

\medterm{promyelocytes} Promyelocytes are immature myeloid blood-cell precursors that develop from myeloblasts and mature into myelocytes; abnormal accumulation occurs in some leukemias.

\medterm{prone} Prone means lying face downward with the front of the body against a surface; positioning affects breathing, pressure injury, and procedures.

\medterm{pronephric} Pronephric refers to the earliest embryonic kidney system, which is temporary and helps organize development of later renal structures.

\medterm{propane} Propane is a flammable hydrocarbon gas used as fuel; inhalation or displacement of oxygen can cause suffocation, and liquid contact can cause cold burns.

\medterm{propanol} Propanol is an alcohol solvent with two structural isomers; ingestion or substantial exposure can depress the nervous system and irritate tissues.

\medterm{propellant} A propellant is a gas or volatile substance that expels a product from a container or delivers medicine in an inhaler.

\medterm{properdin} Properdin is a positive regulator of the alternative complement pathway that stabilizes complement convertase and supports innate immune defense.

\medterm{propetamphos} Propetamphos is an organophosphate insecticide that can inhibit acetylcholinesterase and cause cholinergic toxicity after exposure.

\medterm{prophage} A prophage is bacteriophage genetic material integrated into a bacterial chromosome or maintained in a dormant state inside a bacterium.

\medterm{prophase} Prophase is the early stage of cell division when chromosomes condense, the spindle begins forming, and the nucleolus disappears.

\medterm{prophenofos} Prophenofos is an organophosphate pesticide whose toxicity results mainly from acetylcholinesterase inhibition and excess cholinergic activity.

\medterm{prophylactic mastectomy} prophylactic mastectomy is used in relation to prophylactic mastectomy during assessment, diagnosis, treatment, monitoring, or patient support.

\medterm{propiolactone} Propiolactone is a reactive chemical formerly used as a disinfectant or sterilant; it is corrosive and potentially carcinogenic.

\medterm{propionates} Salts or esters of propionic acid used in food preservation, metabolism, and some medicines; clinical effects depend on the specific compound and exposure.

\medterm{propionibacteriaceae} propionibacteriaceae is a microorganism or microbial group identified by its biologic and taxonomic features. Clinical significance depends on pathogenicity, exposure, the site involved, and antimicrobial susceptibility.

\medterm{propionibacterium} propionibacterium is a microorganism or microbial group identified by its biologic and taxonomic features. Clinical significance depends on pathogenicity, exposure, the site involved, and antimicrobial susceptibility.

\medterm{propionibacterium acnes} propionibacterium acnes is a microorganism or microbial group identified by its biologic and taxonomic features. Clinical significance depends on pathogenicity, exposure, the site involved, and antimicrobial susceptibility.

\medterm{propofol} propofol is a medicine or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dosing, contraindications, interactions, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{propoxur} Propoxur is a carbamate insecticide that inhibits acetylcholinesterase and may cause sweating, salivation, vomiting, breathing difficulty, or weakness after exposure.

\medterm{propoxycaine} propoxycaine is a medication or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{propoxyphene} Propoxyphene is a former opioid analgesic withdrawn in many countries because of overdose toxicity, respiratory depression, and dangerous cardiac arrhythmias.

\medterm{propranolol} propranolol is a medication or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{propylene glycol} A biological substance, cellular process, or molecular structure whose defining role is indicated by the term; its medical significance depends on function, location, amount, and clinical context.

\medterm{prosencephalon} The prosencephalon is the embryonic forebrain, which develops into the telencephalon and diencephalon and their associated brain structures.

\medterm{prosopagnosia} Inability to recognize familiar faces despite adequate vision, caused by developmental differences or injury to face-processing networks, especially in the occipitotemporal cortex.

\medterm{prosoposchisis} A rare congenital facial cleft or fissure caused by abnormal fusion of facial processes during embryonic development, potentially affecting the nose, mouth, eyes, and feeding or breathing.

\medterm{prostaglandin} A locally acting lipid mediator derived from arachidonic acid that influences pain, inflammation, fever, vascular tone, platelet function, gastric protection, kidney function, and uterine contraction.

\medterm{prostaglandin antagonist} A drug that blocks a prostaglandin receptor or pathway, reducing selected prostaglandin-mediated effects such as inflammation, pain, uterine contraction, or gastric secretion.

\medterm{prostaglandin degradation} Enzymatic inactivation and removal of prostaglandins, which limits the duration and intensity of their local effects on vessels, inflammation, smooth muscle, kidneys, stomach, and reproductive tissues.

\medterm{prostaglandin receptor} A membrane receptor that binds a specific prostaglandin and activates intracellular signaling controlling vascular tone, inflammation, pain, smooth muscle, platelet activity, or reproduction.

\medterm{prostaglandins e} The E series of prostaglandins, lipid mediators that influence inflammation, vascular tone, gastric protection, kidney blood flow, cervical ripening, and uterine contraction.

\medterm{prostaglandins f} The F series of prostaglandins, lipid mediators involved in smooth-muscle contraction, luteal function, vascular responses, inflammation, and labor-related uterine activity.

\medterm{prostate carcinoma} prostate carcinoma describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{prostate disease} prostate disease describes a clinical disorder or finding in the body system or process named by the term. Evaluation identifies the underlying mechanism, affected site, severity, and clinically relevant complications.

\medterm{prostate infection} prostate infection describes an infectious or toxic process involving the site or exposure named by the term. Diagnosis considers the cause, route of exposure, affected tissues, severity, and host response.

\medterm{prostate neoplasm} prostate neoplasm describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{prostatic fluid} Prostatic fluid is a mildly alkaline secretion that forms part of semen and contains substances supporting sperm function and survival.

\medterm{prostatic obstruction} prostatic obstruction describes a clinical disorder or finding in the body system or process named by the term. Evaluation identifies the underlying mechanism, affected site, severity, and clinically relevant complications.

\medterm{prostatism} Lower urinary-tract symptoms traditionally attributed to prostate enlargement, including weak stream, hesitancy, frequency, urgency, nocturia, and incomplete emptying; causes are not limited to the prostate.

\medterm{prosthetic heart valve} An artificial valve replacing or supplementing a diseased cardiac valve; mechanical valves require anticoagulation, while tissue valves have different durability and anticoagulation considerations.

\medterm{protamine} Protamine is a positively charged protein used to neutralize heparin and also helps package sperm DNA in sperm cells.

\medterm{protanopia} Protanopia is complete absence of red-cone function, causing a form of red-green color-vision deficiency and altered perception of red and related colors.

\medterm{proteaphagy} proteaphagy is a biochemical, genetic, or chemical term used in medicine; its significance depends on its structure, function, concentration, pathway, or exposure.

\medterm{proteasome} proteasome is a biochemical, genetic, or chemical term used in medicine; its significance depends on its structure, function, concentration, pathway, or exposure.

\medterm{proteasome binding} proteasome binding is a biochemical, genetic, or chemical term used in medicine; its significance depends on its structure, function, concentration, pathway, or exposure.

\medterm{proteasome inhibitor} proteasome inhibitor is a medicine or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{protective agent} protective agent is a biochemical, genetic, or chemical term used in medicine; its significance depends on its structure, function, concentration, pathway, or exposure.

\medterm{protegrin} protegrin is a biochemical, genetic, or chemical term used in medicine; its significance depends on its structure, function, concentration, pathway, or exposure.

\medterm{protein acetylation} Addition of an acetyl group to a protein, often changing its activity, localization, stability, or interactions; histone acetylation is a major regulator of gene expression.

\medterm{protein biosynthesis} Production of proteins by transcription of DNA into RNA followed by ribosomal translation of messenger RNA into an amino-acid chain.

\medterm{protein deficiency} Inadequate protein intake, digestion, absorption, or use, causing loss of muscle, impaired immunity and wound healing, edema, or growth failure when severe or prolonged.

\medterm{protein denaturation} Loss of a protein’s normal three-dimensional structure from heat, chemicals, pH, or mechanical stress, often impairing its biological function without breaking its peptide backbone.

\medterm{protein dephosphorylation} Removal of a phosphate group from a protein by a phosphatase, often reversing or modifying signaling, enzyme activity, localization, or cell-cycle control.

\medterm{protein engineering} Design or modification of proteins through genetic, biochemical, or computational methods to alter activity, stability, specificity, expression, or therapeutic usefulness.

\medterm{protein expression} The process by which genetic information produces a protein, including transcription, RNA processing, translation, folding, modification, and cellular localization.

\medterm{protein glycosylation} Enzymatic attachment of carbohydrate chains to proteins, affecting folding, stability, trafficking, recognition, and function; inherited glycosylation defects can cause multisystem disease.

\medterm{protein import} Transport of a protein into an organelle or across a cellular membrane using targeting signals, receptors, translocons, and energy-dependent folding or assembly mechanisms.

\medterm{protein isoform} One of several related protein forms produced from different genes, alternative splicing, or post-translational processing, with potentially different locations or functions.

\medterm{protein kinase} An enzyme that transfers phosphate from ATP to a protein, changing its activity or interactions and regulating signaling, metabolism, growth, differentiation, and cell division.

\medterm{protein metabolism} The synthesis, folding, modification, breakdown, and recycling of proteins and amino acids in cells and tissues.

\medterm{protein methylation} Addition of methyl groups to selected amino-acid residues, regulating protein activity, chromatin, signaling, and gene expression.

\medterm{protein overexpression} Production of an abnormally large amount of a protein, caused by gene amplification, transcriptional activation, altered degradation, or other mechanisms and potentially driving disease or serving as a biomarker.

\medterm{protein phosphorylation} Addition of phosphate to a protein by a kinase, a reversible switch that regulates enzyme activity, signaling pathways, gene expression, transport, and cell division.

\medterm{protein prenylation} Attachment of a hydrophobic isoprenoid group to a protein, helping target it to membranes and enabling signaling or trafficking functions.

\medterm{protein purification} Separation of a desired protein from cells or a mixture using properties such as charge, size, solubility, affinity, or binding, while preserving its structure and activity.

\medterm{protein refolding} Return of a denatured or newly synthesized protein to its functional three-dimensional structure, sometimes requiring chaperones or controlled laboratory conditions.

\medterm{protein secretion} Transport of a protein out of a cell or into a specialized compartment, commonly through the endoplasmic-reticulum and Golgi pathway or through regulated vesicle release.

\medterm{protein sequence} The ordered chain of amino acids in a protein, which encodes information about folding, domains, binding sites, processing, and biological function.

\medterm{protein splicing} Removal of an internal protein segment called an intein with ligation of the surrounding exteins, producing a mature functional protein.

\medterm{protein subunit} One polypeptide component of a multi-subunit protein complex, whose interactions with other subunits create the complex’s final structure and function.

\medterm{protein translocation} Movement of a newly synthesized or mature protein across a membrane or to a different cellular compartment, directed by targeting signals and transport machinery.

\medterm{protein transport} Cellular movement of proteins between compartments, across membranes, or outside the cell, ensuring that each protein reaches its functional location.

\medterm{protein unfolding} Loss of a protein’s native three-dimensional structure, caused by heat, chemicals, mutations, or cellular stress, which can impair function or promote aggregation.

\medterm{proteinaceous plug} A blockage made primarily of protein-rich material, mucus, cellular debris, or secretions that can obstruct an airway, duct, catheter, or other passage.

\medterm{proteobacteria} proteobacteria is a microorganism or microbial group identified by its biologic and taxonomic features. Clinical significance depends on pathogenicity, exposure, the site involved, and antimicrobial susceptibility.

\medterm{proteogenomics} Integration of genomic, transcriptomic, and proteomic data to identify proteins, discover coding variants, characterize tumors or microbes, and connect DNA changes with protein function.

\medterm{proteoglycan} proteoglycan is a biochemical, genetic, or chemical term used in medicine; its significance depends on its structure, function, concentration, pathway, or exposure.

\medterm{proteolipids} Proteins tightly associated with lipid molecules or lipid membranes, important in myelin, mitochondrial membranes, ion transport, and other membrane structures.

\medterm{proteolysis} proteolysis is a biochemical, genetic, or chemical term used in medicine; its significance depends on its structure, function, concentration, pathway, or exposure.

\medterm{proteome} proteome is a biochemical, genetic, or chemical term used in medicine; its significance depends on its structure, function, concentration, pathway, or exposure.

\medterm{proteomic profile} proteomic profile is a defined medical, biological, or research term; it describes the named process, measurement, structure, or finding and is interpreted with the surrounding clinical information.

\medterm{proteomics} proteomics is a biochemical, genetic, or chemical term used in medicine; its significance depends on its structure, function, concentration, pathway, or exposure.

\medterm{proteus} proteus is a biological organism or infectious agent; its clinical importance depends on the species, host, site, exposure, and evidence of infection or colonization.

\medterm{proteus hauseri} proteus hauseri is a biological organism or infectious agent; its clinical importance depends on the species, host, site, exposure, and evidence of infection or colonization.

\medterm{proteus mirabilis} proteus mirabilis is a biological organism or infectious agent; its clinical importance depends on the species, host, site, exposure, and evidence of infection or colonization.

\medterm{proteus penneri} proteus penneri is a biological organism or infectious agent; its clinical importance depends on the species, host, site, exposure, and evidence of infection or colonization.

\medterm{proteus syndrome} proteus syndrome describes a clinical disorder or finding in the body system or process named by the term. Evaluation identifies the underlying mechanism, affected site, severity, and clinically relevant complications.

\medterm{proteus vulgaris} proteus vulgaris is a biological organism or infectious agent; its clinical importance depends on the species, host, site, exposure, and evidence of infection or colonization.

\medterm{prothionamide} A thioamide antimycobacterial drug used as part of selected multidrug regimens for drug-resistant tuberculosis; gastrointestinal, hepatic, neurologic, and thyroid adverse effects require monitoring.

\medterm{prothrombin} Prothrombin is clotting factor II, a liver-produced vitamin-K-dependent protein converted to thrombin during coagulation.

\medterm{protista} Protista is a historical grouping for diverse mostly single-celled eukaryotes, including organisms formerly called protozoa and some algae.

\medterm{protocol agent} A protocol agent is a substance or intervention specified by a study or treatment protocol to produce a defined experimental or clinical effect.

\medterm{psychoacoustics} The study of how people perceive and interpret sound, including loudness, pitch, masking, localization, speech, and hearing thresholds.

\medterm{psychobiology} The study of interactions between biological processes and behavior or mental states, including brain circuits, hormones, genes, immune signals, and environmental effects.

\medterm{psychodidae} A family of small hairy flies that includes sand flies; some species transmit pathogens such as Leishmania and therefore have public-health importance.

\medterm{psychodrama} A psychotherapy method in which participants enact personal experiences or relationships to explore emotions, perspectives, coping, and interpersonal patterns.

\medterm{psycholinguistics} The study of how the brain and mind produce, understand, acquire, and remember language, including speech, reading, grammar, and meaning.

\medterm{psychological adjustment} The process of adapting thoughts, emotions, and behavior to stress, illness, disability, loss, developmental change, or other life circumstances.

\medterm{psychological dependence} A pattern in which a person feels a strong emotional or mental need for a substance or behavior, often with craving, preoccupation, or distress when it is unavailable.

\medterm{psychological transfer} The redirection of feelings, expectations, or relationship patterns from earlier experiences onto another person, especially the therapist in psychotherapy; interpretation requires clinical context.

\medterm{psychology} The scientific and clinical study of behavior, emotion, cognition, development, personality, and mental processes.

\medterm{psychometrics} The science of measuring psychological traits and states using tests evaluated for reliability, validity, norms, bias, and appropriate interpretation.

\medterm{psychomotor agitation} Restless, excessive, or purposeless movement associated with inner tension, anxiety, mania, delirium, depression, substance effects, or medication reactions.

\medterm{psychomotor disorder} psychomotor disorder describes a clinical disorder or finding in the body system or process named by the term. Evaluation identifies the underlying mechanism, affected site, severity, and clinically relevant complications.

\medterm{psychomotor performance} The ability to coordinate mental processing with physical movement, assessed through tasks involving speed, precision, reaction time, balance, or sequencing.

\medterm{psychoneuroimmunology} The study of interactions among psychological processes, the nervous system, endocrine signals, and immune function in health and disease.

\medterm{psychopathology} The study and description of abnormal mental processes, symptoms, behaviors, and mechanisms underlying psychiatric disorders.

\medterm{psychophysics} The quantitative study of relationships between physical stimuli and subjective sensory experience, such as thresholds, discrimination, and perceived intensity.

\medterm{psychophysiology} The study of links between psychological processes and measurable bodily functions such as heart rate, skin conductance, muscle activity, and brain signals.

\medterm{psychosexual development} Development of sexual identity, interest, relationships, and behavior across the lifespan as influenced by biology, cognition, attachment, culture, and experience.

\medterm{psychosurgery} psychosurgery is used in relation to psychosurgery during assessment, diagnosis, treatment, monitoring, or patient support.

\medterm{Psychotic Disorder} Psychotic Disorder describes a clinical disorder or finding in the body system or process named by the term. Evaluation identifies the underlying mechanism, affected site, severity, and clinically relevant complications.

\medterm{psychotropic drug} psychotropic drug is a medicine or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{psychrobacter} psychrobacter describes an infectious or toxic process involving the site or exposure named by the term. Diagnosis considers the cause, route of exposure, affected tissues, severity, and host response.

\medterm{psychrobacter immobilis} psychrobacter immobilis describes an infectious or toxic process involving the site or exposure named by the term. Diagnosis considers the cause, route of exposure, affected tissues, severity, and host response.

\medterm{pteridine} pteridine is a biochemical, genetic, or chemical term used in medicine; its significance depends on its structure, function, concentration, pathway, or exposure.

\medterm{pubertal failure} Failure to begin or progress through expected puberty because of hypothalamic, pituitary, gonadal, chromosomal, chronic-disease, nutritional, or other causes.

\medterm{public assistance} Government or community support providing eligible people with food, housing, income, healthcare, disability, or other essential services.

\medterm{publication} A formally distributed written, electronic, or multimedia work that communicates research, evidence, education, policy, or other information.

\medterm{puerperal disorder} puerperal disorder describes a clinical disorder or finding in the body system or process named by the term. Evaluation identifies the underlying mechanism, affected site, severity, and clinically relevant complications.

\medterm{puerperal infection} puerperal infection describes an infectious or toxic process involving the site or exposure named by the term. Diagnosis considers the cause, route of exposure, affected tissues, severity, and host response.

\medterm{pulmonary blastoma} pulmonary blastoma describes a neoplastic process involving the tissue or organ named by the term. Diagnosis establishes the tumor type, extent, biologic behavior, and appropriate management.

\medterm{pulmonary circulation} The blood-flow circuit from the right ventricle through the pulmonary arteries and lung capillaries to the left atrium, where blood releases carbon dioxide and gains oxygen.

\medterm{pulmonary compliance} An anatomical structure, tissue, or physiologic process with the location or function indicated by the term; disease or injury changes its expected findings.

\medterm{pulmonary fistula} An anatomical structure, tissue, or physiologic process with the location or function indicated by the term; disease or injury changes its expected findings.

\medterm{pulmonary hemorrhage} pulmonary hemorrhage describes a clinical disorder or finding in the body system or process named by the term. Evaluation identifies the underlying mechanism, affected site, severity, and clinically relevant complications.

\medterm{pulmonary infection} pulmonary infection describes an infectious or toxic process involving the site or exposure named by the term. Diagnosis considers the cause, route of exposure, affected tissues, severity, and host response.

\medterm{pulmonary mass} An anatomical structure, tissue, or physiologic process with the location or function indicated by the term; disease or injury changes its expected findings.

\medterm{pulmonary nodule} An anatomical structure, tissue, or physiologic process with the location or function indicated by the term; disease or injury changes its expected findings.

\medterm{pulmonary reactance} An anatomical structure, tissue, or physiologic process with the location or function indicated by the term; disease or injury changes its expected findings.

\medterm{pulmonary trunk} An anatomical structure, tissue, or physiologic process with the location or function indicated by the term; disease or injury changes its expected findings.

\medterm{pulmonary valve insufficiency} An anatomical structure, tissue, or physiologic process with the location or function indicated by the term; disease or injury changes its expected findings.

\medterm{pulmonologist} A pulmonologist is a physician specializing in diseases of the lungs and breathing system, including asthma, COPD, infections, and sleep-related breathing disorders.

\medterm{pulp canal} The pulp canal is the passage within a tooth root containing dental pulp, nerves, and blood vessels; infection may require root-canal treatment.

\medterm{pulp disease} pulp disease describes a clinical disorder or finding in the body system or process named by the term. Evaluation identifies the underlying mechanism, affected site, severity, and clinically relevant complications.

\medterm{pulpitis} Inflammation of the dental pulp containing nerves and blood vessels, usually caused by deep decay, trauma, or repeated dental procedures and producing toothache or sensitivity.

\medterm{pulse irregular} An irregular pulse has uneven timing or varying beats and may reflect premature beats, atrial fibrillation, other arrhythmias, or measurement variation.

\medterm{pulse radiolysis} Pulse radiolysis is a research technique using short radiation pulses to create and study transient chemical species and reaction kinetics.

\medterm{pulse rate} Pulse rate is the number of palpable arterial beats per minute and usually approximates heart rate, although some arrhythmias produce a pulse deficit.

\medterm{pulvinar} The pulvinar is the largest posterior nucleus of the thalamus and participates in visual attention, sensory integration, and connections with the cortex.

\medterm{puncture biopsy} Removal of a small tissue sample through a needle or puncture for microscopic, molecular, or microbiologic examination; imaging may guide the target and route.

\medterm{punicic acid} A conjugated polyunsaturated fatty acid found mainly in pomegranate seed oil and studied for lipid and inflammatory effects; clinical supplement benefits remain uncertain.

\medterm{punishment} Punishment is a consequence intended to reduce a behavior; in healthcare, coercive or harmful punishment is inappropriate and behavior support should prioritize safety and dignity.

\medterm{purine} purine is a biochemical, genetic, or chemical term used in medicine; its significance depends on its structure, function, concentration, pathway, or exposure.

\medterm{purine nucleosides} purine nucleosides is a biochemical, genetic, or chemical term used in medicine; its significance depends on its structure, function, concentration, pathway, or exposure.

\medterm{purine nucleotides} purine nucleotides is a biochemical, genetic, or chemical term used in medicine; its significance depends on its structure, function, concentration, pathway, or exposure.

\medterm{purinoceptor} A purinoceptor is a cell-surface receptor activated by extracellular purines such as ATP, ADP, or adenosine and involved in signaling, inflammation, and vascular control.

\medterm{purkinje cell} A large inhibitory neuron in the cerebellar cortex whose axons provide the sole output of the cerebellar cortex and regulate coordination, balance, and motor learning.

\medterm{puromycin} puromycin is a medication or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{purulent discharge} purulent discharge is a medical procedure, treatment, device, or care topic; indications, benefits, risks, and follow-up depend on the patient and clinical goal.

\medterm{putamen} The putamen is a basal-ganglia structure involved in movement selection, motor learning, and habit-related behavior.

\medterm{putrescine} Putrescine is a polyamine produced during amino-acid metabolism and decomposition; cells use it in growth, nucleic-acid regulation, and polyamine synthesis.

\medterm{p-value} A p-value is the probability, under a specified null hypothesis, of observing results at least as extreme as those found; it does not by itself measure clinical importance.

\medterm{pycnodysostosis} A rare autosomal-recessive bone dysplasia caused by CTSK deficiency, producing short stature, dense but fragile bones, acro-osteolysis, delayed closure of cranial sutures, and dental abnormalities.

\medterm{pyelitis} Inflammation of the renal pelvis, usually from bacterial urinary infection and often occurring with pyelonephritis; fever, flank pain, dysuria, and urinary testing guide evaluation.

\medterm{pyloric antrum} The pyloric antrum is the lower stomach region that grinds food and regulates its passage toward the pyloric canal and duodenum.

\medterm{pyoderma} A bacterial skin infection producing pus-filled lesions, crusts, ulcers, or deeper inflammation, commonly caused by Staphylococcus or Streptococcus species.

\medterm{pyometra} Accumulation of pus within the uterus, usually from cervical obstruction and infection; it can cause fever, pelvic pain, foul discharge, sepsis, or uterine rupture.

\medterm{pyramidal cell} A triangular-shaped excitatory neuron with a prominent apical dendrite, found especially in the cerebral cortex and hippocampus and involved in long-range brain signaling.

\medterm{pyramidal tract} The pyramidal tract is a major motor pathway from the cerebral cortex to the brainstem and spinal cord that controls voluntary movement.

\medterm{pyrazinamide} pyrazinamide is a medicine or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dosing, contraindications, interactions, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{pyrazines} Pyrazines are aromatic nitrogen-containing chemical compounds found in foods, flavors, medicines, and industrial materials; effects depend on the specific compound and exposure.

\medterm{pyrazole} pyrazole is a medication or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{pyrazoles} pyrazoles is a medicine or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{pyrene} Pyrene is a four-ring polycyclic aromatic hydrocarbon produced by incomplete combustion and used as an environmental marker of complex combustion mixtures.

\medterm{pyrenochaeta} pyrenochaeta is a biological organism or infectious agent; its clinical importance depends on the species, host, site, exposure, and evidence of infection or colonization.

\medterm{pyridine} pyridine is a biochemical, genetic, or chemical term used in medicine; its significance depends on its structure, function, concentration, pathway, or exposure.

\medterm{pyridostigmine bromide} pyridostigmine bromide is a medicine or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dosing, contraindications, interactions, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{pyridoxal phosphate} pyridoxal phosphate is a biochemical, genetic, or chemical term used in medicine; its significance depends on its structure, function, concentration, pathway, or exposure.

\medterm{pyrimethamine} pyrimethamine is a medicine or pharmacologic agent; its indication, dosing, contraindications, interactions, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{pyrimidine analog} pyrimidine analog is a biochemical, genetic, or chemical term used in medicine; its significance depends on its structure, function, concentration, pathway, or exposure.

\medterm{pyrimidine dimer} pyrimidine dimer is a biochemical, genetic, or chemical term used in medicine; its significance depends on its structure, function, concentration, pathway, or exposure.

\medterm{pyrimidine nucleosides} pyrimidine nucleosides is a biochemical, genetic, or chemical term used in medicine; its significance depends on its structure, function, concentration, pathway, or exposure.

\medterm{pyrimidine nucleotides} pyrimidine nucleotides is a biochemical, genetic, or chemical term used in medicine; its significance depends on its structure, function, concentration, pathway, or exposure.

\medterm{pyrogen} A pyrogen is a substance that induces fever, commonly through immune signaling; bacterial endotoxin is an important example in contaminated medicines or fluids.

\medterm{pyroglyphidae} Pyroglyphidae is a mite family that includes house-dust mites whose allergens can trigger allergic rhinitis, asthma, or atopic dermatitis.

\medterm{pyronine} Pyronine is a red fluorescent dye used in histology and research to stain RNA and certain cellular components.

\medterm{pyrophosphatase} pyrophosphatase is a biochemical, genetic, or chemical term used in medicine; its significance depends on its structure, function, concentration, pathway, or exposure.

\medterm{pyropoikilocytosis} A severe inherited red-cell membrane disorder combining marked poikilocytosis, small irregular erythrocytes, thermal instability, and hemolytic anemia.

\medterm{pyroptosis} A programmed inflammatory form of cell death involving gasdermin pores, cell swelling, membrane rupture, and release of cytokines, commonly triggered by intracellular infection or inflammasome activation.

\medterm{pyrosequencing} pyrosequencing is a laboratory test, imaging method, instrument, score, or measurement used to characterize a specimen or clinical finding; interpretation depends on the method and clinical context.

\medterm{pyroxamide} Pyroxamide is an investigational histone-deacetylase inhibitor studied for anticancer effects through altered gene expression and cell growth.

\medterm{pyrroles} pyrroles is a biochemical, genetic, or chemical term used in medicine; its significance depends on its structure, function, concentration, pathway, or exposure.

\medterm{pyrrolidonecarboxylic acid} A cyclic amino-acid derivative, also called pyroglutamic acid, found in proteins and metabolic pathways; abnormal accumulation may occur in selected metabolic or medication-related conditions.

\medterm{pyrrolnitrin} Pyrrolnitrin is an antimicrobial compound produced by some bacteria and studied for antifungal activity and biological control.

\medterm{pyruvate decarboxylase} Pyruvate decarboxylase is an enzyme that converts pyruvate to acetaldehyde and carbon dioxide during fermentation in some organisms.

\medterm{pyruvate kinase} Pyruvate kinase catalyzes the final ATP-producing step of glycolysis; inherited deficiency causes hemolytic anemia because red cells cannot generate enough energy.

\medterm{Painful urination (dysuria)} Pain, burning, or discomfort during urination, commonly caused by urinary infection, inflammation, stones, irritation, or sexually transmitted infection.

\medterm{Peeling skin} Loss or shedding of the outer layer of skin, which may result from dryness, sunburn, irritation, allergy, infection, inflammation, or a systemic illness.

\medterm{Protein in urine (proteinuria)} An abnormal amount of protein in the urine, which may be temporary or indicate kidney disease, diabetes, high blood pressure, infection, or another condition.

\medterm{Point tenderness - abdomen} Abdominal point tenderness is sharply localized pain when a particular abdominal site is pressed, which may indicate inflammation, infection, obstruction, or another focal process.

\medterm{Poison control center - emergency number} A poison-control center provides immediate expert advice after a possible poisoning or toxic exposure; the correct emergency number depends on the country and life-threatening symptoms require emergency services.

\medterm{Poison ivy - oak - sumac} Poison ivy, oak, and sumac cause allergic contact dermatitis through urushiol oil, producing intensely itchy red patches and blisters after skin exposure.

\medterm{Poison ivy - oak - sumac rash} A poison-ivy, oak, or sumac rash is itchy allergic contact dermatitis caused by urushiol, often appearing in streaks and spreading as exposed oil remains on skin or clothing.

\medterm{Poisoning - fish and shellfish} Poisoning - fish and shellfish involves toxic or environmental exposure to poisoning - fish and shellfish; dose, route, timing, symptoms, and organ involvement determine urgency and treatment.

\medterm{Poisoning first aid} Poisoning first aid involves toxic or environmental exposure to poisoning first aid; dose, route, timing, symptoms, and organ involvement determine urgency and treatment.

\medterm{Pokeweed poisoning} Pokeweed poisoning involves toxic or environmental exposure to pokeweed poisoning; dose, route, timing, symptoms, and organ involvement determine urgency and treatment.

\medterm{Polio vaccine - what you need to know} Polio vaccine - what you need to know is a procedure, device, treatment, or care intervention; indications, benefits, risks, and follow-up depend on the patient and clinical goal.

\medterm{Polychromatophilia} Polychromatophilia is bluish-gray staining of red blood cells on a blood smear, reflecting increased circulating immature reticulocytes from stimulated red-cell production.

\medterm{Polycythemia - newborn} Neonatal polycythemia is an abnormally high red-cell concentration in a newborn that can increase blood viscosity and cause poor feeding, lethargy, breathing problems, or low glucose.

\medterm{Polyendocrine metabolic ovarian syndrome} A disease, symptom, syndrome, or clinical finding involving the body system or process named by the term; characteristic features, causes, severity, and complications guide diagnosis and management.

\medterm{Polymorphous light eruption} Polymorphous light eruption is an itchy inflammatory rash triggered by sunlight, usually appearing hours to days after exposure and recurring seasonally in susceptible people.

\medterm{Polymyositis - adult} Adult polymyositis is an uncommon immune-mediated inflammatory muscle disease causing progressive symmetric proximal weakness and elevated muscle enzymes, after exclusion of mimics.

\medterm{Polyp biopsy} Polyp biopsy examines polyp biopsy; interpretation depends on the specimen or body region, test method, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Pompholyx eczema} Pompholyx, or dyshidrotic eczema, causes intensely itchy small blisters on the palms, sides of fingers, or soles and may leave painful peeling or cracks.

\medterm{Porphyrins blood test} Porphyrins blood test examines porphyrins blood test; interpretation depends on the specimen or body region, test method, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Porphyrins urine test} Porphyrins urine test examines porphyrins urine test; interpretation depends on the specimen or body region, test method, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Portacaval shunting} Portacaval shunting is a procedure, device, treatment, or care intervention; indications, benefits, risks, and follow-up depend on the patient and clinical goal.

\medterm{Portion size} The amount of food or drink served or eaten at one time, distinct from a standardized serving size and important for estimating energy, carbohydrate, sodium, and nutrient intake.

\medterm{Positioning your baby for breastfeeding} Placing an infant’s head, neck, and body in alignment with the breast so the mouth opens widely, the latch is deep, and feeding is comfortable and effective.

\medterm{Positive airway pressure treatment} Positive airway pressure treatment is a medical procedure, treatment, device, or care topic; indications, benefits, risks, and follow-up depend on the patient and clinical goal.

\medterm{Post-splenectomy complications} Post-splenectomy complications examines post-splenectomy complications; interpretation depends on the specimen or body region, test method, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Postherpetic neuralgia - aftercare} Postherpetic neuralgia - aftercare examines postherpetic neuralgia - aftercare; interpretation depends on the specimen or body region, test method, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Poststreptococcal glomerulonephritis (GN)} Poststreptococcal glomerulonephritis is kidney inflammation occurring after certain streptococcal infections, causing blood or protein in urine, swelling, high blood pressure, and reduced kidney function.

\medterm{Postsurgical pain treatment in adults} Postsurgical pain treatment in adults addresses care related to postsurgical pain treatment in adults, including practical measures, warning signs, follow-up, and decisions to discuss with a clinician.

\medterm{Potassium hydroxide poisoning} Potassium hydroxide poisoning involves toxic or environmental exposure to potassium hydroxide poisoning; dose, route, timing, symptoms, and organ involvement determine urgency and treatment.
