\medterm{Potato plant poisoning - green tubers and sprouts} Potato plant poisoning - green tubers and sprouts involves toxic or environmental exposure to potato plant poisoning - green tubers and sprouts; dose, route, timing, symptoms, and organ involvement determine urgency and treatment.

\medterm{Potbellies and toddlers} A rounded abdomen in a toddler is often normal body shape or posture, but persistent distension with pain, vomiting, poor growth, constipation, or a mass needs medical assessment.

\medterm{Potential acuity meter} Potential acuity meter is a laboratory test, imaging method, instrument, score, or measurement used to characterize a specimen or clinical finding; interpretation depends on the method and clinical context.

\medterm{PPD skin test} PPD skin test examines ppd skin test; interpretation depends on the specimen or body region, test method, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Pre-existing diabetes and pregnancy} Pre-existing type 1 or type 2 diabetes during pregnancy increases risks to parent and fetus, so glucose control, medicines, fetal growth, blood pressure, and eye and kidney health need coordinated care.

\medterm{Preeclampsia - self-care} Preeclampsia - self-care is a medical procedure, treatment, device, or care topic; indications, benefits, risks, and follow-up depend on the patient and clinical goal.

\medterm{Pregnancy - health risks} Health risks in pregnancy vary with medical conditions, medicines, infections, age, exposures, and obstetric history; prenatal assessment identifies preventable complications and needed monitoring.

\medterm{Pregnancy - identifying fertile days} Fertile days are the interval around ovulation when intercourse can lead to pregnancy; cycle timing, cervical mucus, basal temperature, and ovulation tests can provide estimates but are imperfect.

\medterm{Pregnancy and herpes} Genital herpes during pregnancy can affect delivery planning and rarely infect the newborn; antiviral treatment and examination of lesions near labor help reduce transmission risk.

\medterm{Pregnancy and the flu} Influenza during pregnancy can be more severe and affect the fetus; vaccination is recommended in pregnancy, and suspected illness should receive prompt clinical advice about antiviral treatment.

\medterm{Pregnancy and travel} Travel during pregnancy depends on gestational age, health, destination infections, transport, and access to care; prevention includes vaccines as appropriate, food and water safety, movement, and medical planning.

\medterm{Pregnancy and work} Work during pregnancy is usually possible but may require adjustments for heavy lifting, prolonged standing, heat, chemicals, radiation, infection, night work, or other occupational hazards.

\medterm{Pregnancy care} Pregnancy care addresses care related to pregnancy care, including practical measures, warning signs, follow-up, and decisions to discuss with a clinician.

\medterm{Premature infant} An infant born before 37 completed weeks of gestation, with risks related to immature lungs, brain, feeding, temperature control, immunity, and other organ systems.

\medterm{Premenstrual breast changes} Premenstrual breast changes include cyclic fullness, tenderness, swelling, or lumpiness caused by hormonal variation; a persistent one-sided mass, skin change, or bloody discharge needs evaluation.

\medterm{Premenstrual syndrome - self-care} Premenstrual syndrome - self-care is a medical procedure, treatment, device, or care topic; indications, benefits, risks, and follow-up depend on the patient and clinical goal.

\medterm{Prenatal care in your second trimester} Prenatal care in your second trimester addresses care related to prenatal care in your second trimester, including practical measures, warning signs, follow-up, and decisions to discuss with a clinician.

\medterm{Prenatal care in your third trimester} Prenatal care in your third trimester addresses care related to prenatal care in your third trimester, including practical measures, warning signs, follow-up, and decisions to discuss with a clinician.

\medterm{Preparing children for pregnancy and a new baby} Preparing a child for a new baby involves age-appropriate explanation, routine and attention planning, safe involvement, reassurance, and support for emotional or behavioral changes.

\medterm{Preparing for surgery when you have diabetes} Preparing for surgery when you have diabetes is a medical procedure, treatment, device, or care topic; indications, benefits, risks, and follow-up depend on the patient and clinical goal.

\medterm{Preschooler development} Preschooler development includes rapid gains in language, motor skills, thinking, self-care, play, and social-emotional regulation; milestones vary, but loss of skills warrants assessment.

\medterm{Preschooler test or procedure preparation} Preschooler test or procedure preparation examines preschooler test or procedure preparation; interpretation depends on the specimen or body region, test method, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Pressure injuries - what to ask your doctor} Pressure injuries - what to ask your doctor examines pressure injuries - what to ask your doctor; interpretation depends on the specimen or body region, test method, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Preventing falls} Fall prevention combines strength and balance activity, vision and medication review, safe footwear, adequate lighting, handrails, removal of hazards, and appropriate assistive devices.

\medterm{Preventing falls - what to ask your doctor} Preventing falls - what to ask your doctor examines preventing falls - what to ask your doctor; interpretation depends on the specimen or body region, test method, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Preventing food poisoning} Preventing food poisoning involves toxic or environmental exposure to preventing food poisoning; dose, route, timing, symptoms, and organ involvement determine urgency and treatment.

\medterm{Preventing head injuries in children} Children’s head injuries can be reduced with age-appropriate car seats, helmets, supervision, safe play areas, window and stair protection, and prevention of abusive shaking or impact.

\medterm{Preventing hepatitis A} Hepatitis A prevention relies on vaccination, handwashing, safe food and water, sanitation, and post-exposure advice when close contact with an infected person occurs.

\medterm{Preventing hepatitis B or C} Hepatitis B prevention includes vaccination and avoiding blood or sexual exposure; hepatitis C prevention centers on sterile injection equipment, screened blood, and testing and treatment of infection.

\medterm{Preventing infections when visiting someone in the hospital} Preventing infections when visiting someone in the hospital examines preventing infections when visiting someone in the hospital; interpretation depends on the specimen or body region, test method, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Preventing pressure injuries} Pressure-injury prevention uses regular repositioning, skin inspection, moisture control, nutrition, pressure-redistributing surfaces, and minimizing friction and shear in people with limited mobility.

\medterm{Preventing stroke} Stroke prevention includes controlling blood pressure, diabetes and cholesterol, avoiding tobacco, treating atrial fibrillation when indicated, exercising, and addressing other vascular risks.

\medterm{Preventive health care} Preventive health care addresses care related to preventive health care, including practical measures, warning signs, follow-up, and decisions to discuss with a clinician.

\medterm{Primary alveolar hypoventilation} A rare disorder of inadequate automatic breathing, often related to PHOX2B or other autonomic-control abnormalities, causing elevated carbon dioxide especially during sleep.

\medterm{Primary amyloidosis} Primary amyloidosis, usually AL amyloidosis, results from abnormal plasma-cell proteins depositing as amyloid in organs such as the heart, kidneys, nerves, or gastrointestinal tract.

\medterm{Primary and secondary hyperaldosteronism} Excess aldosterone production, from an adrenal source in primary disease or from increased renin stimulation in secondary disease, causing hypertension, sodium retention, and sometimes low potassium.

\medterm{Primary lymphoma of the brain} Primary central-nervous-system lymphoma is a lymphoma arising in the brain, spinal cord, eyes, or surrounding fluid without systemic disease at diagnosis.

\medterm{Problems sleeping during pregnancy} Sleep problems during pregnancy may result from discomfort, reflux, urination, anxiety, hormonal change, or sleep apnea; safe sleep habits and clinician-guided treatment are important.

\medterm{Prochlorperazine overdose} Prochlorperazine overdose involves toxic or environmental exposure to prochlorperazine overdose; dose, route, timing, symptoms, and organ involvement determine urgency and treatment.

\medterm{Prognathism} Forward projection of the upper or lower jaw relative to the facial skeleton, affecting bite, appearance, chewing, speech, and sometimes airway function.

\medterm{Progressive multifocal leukoencephalopathy} Progressive multifocal leukoencephalopathy is a potentially fatal demyelinating brain infection caused by JC virus reactivation during significant immune suppression.

\medterm{Prolactin blood test} Prolactin blood test examines prolactin blood test; interpretation depends on the specimen or body region, test method, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Promethazine overdose} Promethazine overdose involves toxic or environmental exposure to promethazine overdose; dose, route, timing, symptoms, and organ involvement determine urgency and treatment.

\medterm{Propane poisoning} Propane poisoning involves toxic or environmental exposure to propane poisoning; dose, route, timing, symptoms, and organ involvement determine urgency and treatment.

\medterm{Propyl alcohol} Propyl alcohols are flammable solvents; ingestion or substantial exposure can irritate tissues and depress the central nervous system, with toxicity depending on the isomer and dose.

\medterm{Prostate brachytherapy} Prostate brachytherapy is a medical procedure, treatment, device, or care topic; indications, benefits, risks, and follow-up depend on the patient and clinical goal.

\medterm{Prosthetic graft} A prosthetic graft is an artificial tube or patch used to replace or bypass a damaged blood vessel or other tubular structure; infection and thrombosis are important risks.

\medterm{Protecting yourself from cancer scams} Protecting yourself from cancer scams examines protecting yourself from cancer scams; interpretation depends on the specimen or body region, test method, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Protein-losing enteropathy} An anatomical structure, tissue, or physiologic process with the location or function indicated by the term; disease or injury changes its expected findings.

\medterm{Prothrombin deficiency} Prothrombin deficiency is a rare bleeding disorder caused by insufficient or abnormal clotting factor II, leading to prolonged bleeding of variable severity.

\medterm{Proton pump inhibitors} Proton pump inhibitors suppress gastric acid secretion and treat reflux, ulcers, and other acid-related disorders; prolonged or inappropriate use should be periodically reviewed.

\medterm{Protuberance} A protuberance is a structure or area that projects outward from a surface, such as a normal bone prominence, swelling, or mass.

\medterm{Provisional tic disorder} A disease, symptom, syndrome, or clinical finding involving the body system or process named by the term; characteristic features, causes, severity, and complications guide diagnosis and management.

\medterm{Proximal renal tubular acidosis} A biological substance, cellular process, or molecular structure whose defining role is indicated by the term; its medical significance depends on function, location, amount, and clinical context.

\medterm{Psoriasis - resources} Psoriasis - resources addresses care related to psoriasis - resources, including practical measures, warning signs, follow-up, and decisions to discuss with a clinician.

\medterm{Ptosis - infants and children} Ptosis in an infant or child is drooping of the upper eyelid, which may obstruct vision or cause amblyopia and can reflect congenital, muscular, nerve, or other disease.

\medterm{Puberty in boys} Puberty in boys is the hormone-driven development of testes, penis, body hair, voice, muscle, and fertility, usually progressing through predictable physical stages.

\medterm{Puberty in girls} Puberty in girls is the hormone-driven development of breasts, body hair, growth, menstrual cycles, and reproductive maturity, usually progressing through predictable stages.

\medterm{Pubic lice} Pubic lice are parasitic insects infesting coarse body hair, causing intense itching and visible nits; treatment and assessment of close or sexual contacts are needed.

\medterm{Pulling a patient up in bed} A safe repositioning technique using adequate staff, friction-reducing equipment, and coordinated movement to move a patient toward the head of the bed while preventing falls, shear, and staff injury.

\medterm{Pulmonic valve stenosis} Pulmonic valve stenosis is narrowing of the valve between the right ventricle and pulmonary artery, creating outflow obstruction and sometimes right-heart strain.

\medterm{Pulse - bounding} A bounding pulse feels unusually strong or full and can occur with fever, anemia, hyperthyroidism, exercise, anxiety, aortic regurgitation, or high-output states.

\medterm{Pupil - white} A white pupil, or leukocoria, may result from reflection, cataract, retinal disease, infection, or retinoblastoma and requires prompt eye assessment, especially in a child.

\medterm{Pustules} Pustules are small raised skin lesions filled with pus, occurring in acne, folliculitis, infections, inflammatory disorders, or drug reactions.

\medterm{Pyloric stenosis in infants} Pyloric stenosis in infants examines pyloric stenosis in infants; interpretation depends on the specimen or body region, test method, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Pyloroplasty} Pyloroplasty is surgery that widens the pylorus to improve passage of stomach contents, used in selected cases of gastric-outlet obstruction or impaired emptying.

\medterm{Pyogenic liver abscess} A pyogenic liver abscess is a pus-filled infection in the liver, usually from biliary, abdominal, or bloodstream spread, causing fever, pain, and possible sepsis.

\medterm{Pyruvate kinase blood test} Pyruvate kinase blood test examines pyruvate kinase blood test; interpretation depends on the specimen or body region, test method, reference range, and clinical question.

\medterm{Palpitations Overview} Palpitations are awareness of a racing, pounding, fluttering, or skipped heartbeat. They may result from normal stress, stimulants, anemia, thyroid disease, medicines, or arrhythmia; fainting, chest pain, breathlessness, or sustained rapid rhythm requires urgent assessment.

\medterm{Periodic Paralysis Syndrome} Periodic paralysis syndromes are rare inherited or acquired disorders causing episodic muscle weakness associated with abnormal potassium handling or thyroid disease. Attacks may follow rest after exercise, meals, or stress; ECG, potassium testing, and genetic or endocrine evaluation guide care.

\medterm{Phlebitis and Thrombophlebitis} Phlebitis is inflammation of a vein, while thrombophlebitis includes a clot within the inflamed vein. Superficial disease causes localized pain and redness; deep-vein thrombosis can embolize to the lungs and is suspected with limb swelling, pain, or breathlessness.

\medterm{piles (Hemorrhoids (Piles))} Alternate index label for Hemorrhoids (Piles).

\medterm{pilonidal sinus (Pilonidal Cyst)} Alternate index label for Pilonidal Cyst.

\medterm{Pinched Nerve Overview} A pinched nerve is compression or irritation of a nerve root or peripheral nerve, causing pain, tingling, numbness, weakness, or radiating symptoms. Causes include disc disease, narrowing, injury, or entrapment; progressive weakness or bowel or bladder change is urgent.

\medterm{Pinkeye Contagious (Is Pinkeye Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is Pinkeye Contagious.

\medterm{Pinky Broken Finger (Broken Finger)} Alternate index label for Broken Finger.

\medterm{Pinworm Infection} Pinworm infection is an intestinal infestation by Enterobius vermicularis, causing nighttime anal itching, disturbed sleep, and sometimes abdominal or genital irritation. Treatment of the person and close household contacts, hygiene, and repeat dosing reduce reinfection.

\medterm{PiriformSyndrome (Piriformis Syndrome)} Alternate index label for Piriformis Syndrome.

\medterm{Pituitary Tumor (Prolactinoma)} Alternate index label for Prolactinoma.

\medterm{pityriasis rosea gibert (Pityriasis Rosea)} Alternate index label for Pityriasis Rosea.

\medterm{Plague (Plague Facts)} Alternate index label for Plague Facts.

\medterm{Plague Facts} Plague is an infection caused by Yersinia pestis, usually acquired from infected flea exposure or animals. Bubonic disease causes painful lymph nodes, while pneumonic disease can spread through respiratory droplets; prompt antibiotics and public-health action are essential.

\medterm{plasmodium infection (Malaria Facts)} Alternate index label for Malaria Facts.

\medterm{Platelet Count Low (Thrombocytopenia (Low Platelet Count))} Alternate index label for Thrombocytopenia (Low Platelet Count).

\medterm{Platelet Disorder - Immune Thrombocytopenia (ITP)} Alternate index label for ITP.

\medterm{Pleural Effusion (Fluid In the Chest or On Lung)} Alternate index label for Fluid In the Chest or On Lung.

\medterm{Pleurisy Contagious (Is Pleurisy Contagious)} Alternate index label for Is Pleurisy Contagious.

\medterm{pleuritis (Pleurisy)} Alternate index label for Pleurisy.

\medterm{PM (Polymyositis)} Alternate index label for Polymyositis.

\medterm{PMDD (Premenstrual Dysphoric Disorder (PMDD))} Alternate index label for Premenstrual Dysphoric Disorder (PMDD).

\medterm{PMR (Polymyalgia Rheumatica)} Alternate index label for Polymyalgia Rheumatica.

\medterm{Poison Ivy, Oak, and Sumac} Poison ivy, oak, and sumac cause allergic contact dermatitis through urushiol oil, producing intensely itchy streaks or patches of redness, blisters, and swelling. Washing exposed skin and clothing promptly helps; breathing difficulty or facial swelling is an emergency.

\medterm{Poisoning Arsenic (Arsenic Poisoning)} Alternate index label for Arsenic Poisoning.

\medterm{Poisoning Ciguatera (Ciguatera Poisoning)} Alternate index label for Ciguatera Poisoning.

\medterm{Poisoning Thallium (Thallium)} Alternate index label for Thallium.

\medterm{Polio Facts} Polio is infection with poliovirus, which may be asymptomatic or cause meningitis, muscle weakness, paralysis, and respiratory failure. Vaccination is highly effective prevention; suspected disease requires urgent medical and public-health evaluation.

\medterm{Polycystic Ovary} Polycystic ovaries contain many small follicles and may occur as a normal variant or with polycystic ovary syndrome. PCOS additionally involves ovulatory dysfunction, androgen excess, irregular periods, infertility, acne, or metabolic risk and requires clinical—not ultrasound alone—diagnosis.

\medterm{Polycythemia (High Red Blood Cell Count)} Alternate index label for High Red Blood Cell Count.

\medterm{Polyps Colon (Colon Polyps)} Alternate index label for Colon Polyps.

\medterm{Polyps Rectal (Colon Polyps)} Alternate index label for Colon Polyps.

\medterm{Polyps Uterus (Uterine Fibroids)} Alternate index label for Uterine Fibroids.

\medterm{Popcorn Lung Bronchiolitis Obliterans} Bronchiolitis obliterans is irreversible scarring and narrowing of small airways that can follow toxic inhalation, infection, transplantation, or autoimmune disease. It causes persistent cough and breathlessness with obstructive lung testing; exposure avoidance and specialist care are important.

\medterm{popliteal cyst (Baker Cyst)} Alternate index label for Baker Cyst.

\medterm{Post-Nasal Drip (Chronic Rhinitis)} Alternate index label for Chronic Rhinitis.

\medterm{Pot Marijuana (Marijuana)} Alternate index label for Marijuana.

\medterm{Powassan Virus Disease} Powassan virus disease is a rare tick-borne infection that can cause fever, headache, vomiting, confusion, seizures, meningitis, or encephalitis. Treatment is supportive, and prevention relies on avoiding ticks and removing them promptly.

\medterm{PPD (Postpartum Depression)} Alternate index label for Postpartum Depression.

\medterm{Pregnancy Diet (Pregnancy: Your Guide to Eating Right)} Alternate index label for Pregnancy: Your Guide to Eating Right.

\medterm{Pregnancy Ectopic (Ectopic Pregnancy)} Alternate index label for Ectopic Pregnancy.

\medterm{Pregnancy High Blood Pressure (Pregnancy: Preeclampsia and Eclampsia)} Alternate index label for Pregnancy: Preeclampsia and Eclampsia.

\medterm{Pregnancy Planning (Preparing for Pregnancy)} Alternate index label for Preparing for Pregnancy.

\medterm{Pregnancy Tubal (Ectopic Pregnancy)} Alternate index label for Ectopic Pregnancy.

\medterm{Pregnancy vs. PMS (PMS vs. Pregnancy Differences and Similarities)} Alternate index label for PMS vs. Pregnancy Differences and Similarities.

\medterm{Pregnancy: Bleeding During the First Trimester} First-trimester bleeding may occur with implantation, cervical changes, miscarriage, ectopic pregnancy, or other causes. Assessment uses symptoms, examination, serial pregnancy hormone testing, and ultrasound; heavy bleeding, severe pain, faintness, or shoulder pain is urgent.

\medterm{Pregnancy: Pain Relief Options for Birth} Labor pain relief may include breathing and movement, water, massage, nitrous oxide, intravenous medicines, epidural analgesia, or spinal anesthesia. Choice depends on labor stage, maternal and fetal status, contraindications, availability, and informed preference.

\medterm{Pregnancy: Placenta Previa} Placenta previa occurs when the placenta lies over or near the cervical opening and can cause painless bright-red bleeding, especially later in pregnancy. Digital vaginal examination is avoided until location is known; recurrent bleeding requires obstetric planning and sometimes cesarean delivery.

\medterm{Pregnancy: Preeclampsia and Eclampsia} Preeclampsia is new hypertension during pregnancy with proteinuria or organ dysfunction; eclampsia is the occurrence of seizures. Severe headache, visual symptoms, upper-abdominal pain, shortness of breath, or seizure requires emergency obstetric treatment.

\medterm{Pregnant (Pregnancy Planning (Preparing for Pregnancy))} Alternate index label for Pregnancy Planning (Preparing for Pregnancy).

\medterm{Premature Menopause (Medical Procedural Causes)} Alternate index label for Medical Procedural Causes.

\medterm{Premenstrual Dysphoric Disorder (PMDD)} Alternate index label for PMDD.

\medterm{Premenstrual Syndrome (Menstrual Cramps and PMS Medication Guide)} Alternate index label for Menstrual Cramps and PMS Medication Guide.

\medterm{Prevention IBS (Irritable Bowel Syndrome (IBS) Triggers, Prevention)} Alternate index label for Irritable Bowel Syndrome (IBS) Triggers, Prevention.

\medterm{Priapism (Penis Disorder)} Alternate index label for Penis Disorder.

\medterm{Primary Biliary Cirrhosis (PBC)} Alternate index label for PBC.

\medterm{Primary Biliary Cirrhosis Treatment (PBC)} Alternate index label for PBC.

\medterm{Primary Immunodeficiency Disease PIDD} Primary immunodeficiency diseases are inherited disorders of immune development or function that cause unusual, severe, recurrent, or persistent infections and sometimes autoimmunity or inflammation. Evaluation may include blood counts, immunoglobulins, functional tests, and genetic testing.

\medterm{Primary Sclerosing Cholangitis (PSC)} Alternate index label for PSC.

\medterm{Primary vs. Secondary Progressive MS} Primary progressive multiple sclerosis worsens from onset without clear early relapses, while secondary progressive MS follows an earlier relapsing course and later develops steady progression. Disability, MRI activity, relapses, and treatment response guide management.

\medterm{Profunda Miliaria (Heat Rash)} Alternate index label for Heat Rash.

\medterm{prostate inflammation (Prostatitis (Inflammation of the Prostate Gland))} Alternate index label for Prostatitis (Inflammation of the Prostate Gland).

\medterm{prostatic carcinoma (Prostate Cancer)} Alternate index label for Prostate Cancer.

\medterm{Prostatitis (Inflammation of the Prostate Gland)} Alternate index label for Inflammation of the Prostate Gland.

\medterm{Prostatitis vs BPH Enlarged Prostate Gland} Prostatitis is inflammation or infection of the prostate and may cause pelvic pain, painful urination, fever, or urinary symptoms. Benign prostatic hyperplasia is age-related prostate enlargement causing weak stream, hesitancy, and incomplete emptying without infection.

\medterm{Proximal Neuropathy Diabetic (Diabetic Neuropathy)} Alternate index label for Diabetic Neuropathy.

\medterm{Pruritus (Itch)} Alternate index label for Itch.

\medterm{PSC (Primary Sclerosing Cholangitis (PSC))} Alternate index label for Primary Sclerosing Cholangitis (PSC).

\medterm{Psoriasis of the scalp (Scalp Psoriasis)} Alternate index label for Scalp Psoriasis.

\medterm{PSVT (Paroxysmal Supraventricular Tachycardia (PSVT))} Alternate index label for Paroxysmal Supraventricular Tachycardia (PSVT).

\medterm{Psychosis ICU (ICU Psychosis)} Alternate index label for ICU Psychosis.

\medterm{Psychotic Disorder Brief (Brief Psychotic Disorder)} Alternate index label for Brief Psychotic Disorder.

\medterm{Psychotic Disorders} Psychotic disorders involve impaired reality testing, such as hallucinations, delusions, disorganized thought, or marked behavioral change. Causes include schizophrenia-spectrum illness, mood disorders, substances, medicines, neurologic disease, and medical illness; new psychosis requires prompt assessment and safety planning.

\medterm{PTSD (Posttraumatic Stress Disorder)} Alternate index label for Posttraumatic Stress Disorder.

\medterm{Pugilistica Dementia (Dementia)} Alternate index label for Dementia.

\medterm{Pulled Hamstring (Hamstring Injury)} Alternate index label for Hamstring Injury.

\medterm{Pulmonary FibrosIdiopathic (Idiopathic Pulmonary Fibrosis (IPF))} Alternate index label for Idiopathic Pulmonary Fibrosis (IPF).

\medterm{PVC (Premature Ventricular Contractions)} Alternate index label for Premature Ventricular Contractions.

\medterm{PVD (Peripheral Vascular Disease)} Alternate index label for Peripheral Vascular Disease.

\medterm{PXE (Pseudoxanthoma Elasticum)} Alternate index label for Pseudoxanthoma Elasticum.

\medterm{p arm of a chromosome} The short arm of a chromosome, designated p for petit, opposite the longer q arm; cytogenetic locations are written with p-band coordinates.

\medterm{p.c.} P.C. is an abbreviation whose meaning depends on context; in medication instructions it commonly means after meals, while other clinical uses must be specified.

\medterm{p.o.} P.O. is the medication abbreviation for by mouth or orally; clear modern instructions should spell this out to prevent route errors.

\medterm{p.r.n.} P.R.N. means as needed, indicating that a medicine or intervention is used only when specified symptoms or circumstances occur within prescribed limits.

\medterm{PA (physician assistant)} A physician assistant is a licensed healthcare professional who evaluates patients, diagnoses and treats illness, prescribes where authorized, and works within a supervising or collaborative practice model.

\medterm{PA X-ray} A PA X-ray is a posteroanterior radiograph, commonly of the chest, taken with the beam traveling from back to front to reduce heart magnification.

\medterm{Pachy- (prefix)} A word-forming prefix meaning thick or thickness, as in pachyonychia, pachyderma, and pachycephaly.

\medterm{Pachyderma} Abnormal thickening and coarsening of the skin, which may occur with chronic inflammation, lymphedema, endocrine disease, or pachydermoperiostosis.

\medterm{Paget disease} A disease, symptom, syndrome, or clinical finding involving the body system or process named by the term; characteristic features, causes, severity, and complications guide diagnosis and management.

\medterm{Pain unit} A clinical service specializing in assessment and treatment of acute, chronic, cancer, neuropathic, or procedural pain using medicines, procedures, rehabilitation, and psychological care.

\medterm{Painful urination} Dysuria, or pain, burning, or discomfort during urination, commonly caused by urinary infection, urethritis, stones, irritation, or inflammatory genital disease.

\medterm{Paleostriatum} The paleostriatum is a historical or comparative-anatomy term for a forebrain basal-ganglia region involved in motor control, corresponding in part to mammalian pallidal structures.

\medterm{Palindrome} A palindrome is a word or sequence that reads the same forward and backward; in genetics, palindromic DNA sequences can have specialized structural or recognition roles.

\medterm{Palliative treatment} Palliative treatment is a medical test, procedure, treatment, or clinical measure used in patient care; its purpose and interpretation depend on the clinical question.

\medterm{Palm} The palm is the front surface of the hand between the wrist and fingers, containing skin, fascia, muscles, nerves, vessels, and tendon structures.

\medterm{Palmar} Palmar means relating to or located on the palm side of the hand.

\medterm{Palmitate} Palmitate is the salt or ester form of palmitic acid and participates in fatty-acid metabolism, lipid storage, and protein modification.

\medterm{Palpitations} The conscious sensation of the heartbeat as pounding, racing, fluttering, or skipping, caused by normal beats, arrhythmias, stimulants, anxiety, anemia, thyroid disease, or other conditions.

\medterm{Panic} Panic is sudden intense fear or distress with autonomic symptoms such as racing heart, sweating, trembling, breathlessness, or a sense of impending danger.

\medterm{Panniculus} A panniculus is a fold or layer of subcutaneous fatty tissue, especially the lower abdominal apron that may overhang the pubic region.

\medterm{PAP} PAP is an abbreviation with context-dependent meanings, including Papanicolaou testing, pulmonary alveolar proteinosis, or prostatic acid phosphatase; the full term should be specified.

\medterm{Para} Para- is a prefix meaning beside, beyond, abnormal, or related to childbirth depending on the word; its exact meaning must be read in context.

\medterm{Para- (prefix)} Para- is a medical prefix whose meaning varies by use, including beside, beyond, abnormal, or the number of births reaching a specified gestational age.

\medterm{Parainfectious} Parainfectious describes a condition occurring during or around an infection, often from an immune response rather than direct invasion by the infectious organism.

\medterm{Paralysis, stomach} Stomach paralysis, or gastroparesis, is delayed gastric emptying without a mechanical blockage, causing early fullness, nausea, vomiting, bloating, and variable glucose control.

\medterm{Paralytic ileus} Paralytic ileus is temporary failure of intestinal movement without a physical obstruction, often after surgery, inflammation, metabolic disturbance, or medicines such as opioids.

\medterm{Parasitic} Parasitic means relating to an organism that lives in or on a host and obtains nutrients at the host’s expense, sometimes causing disease.

\medterm{Parasitic worm} A parasitic worm is a helminth that lives in a host; human infection may involve the intestine, blood, lymphatics, lungs, liver, or tissues.

\medterm{Parasitosis} Infestation or infection by a parasite; the term may describe a confirmed parasitic disease or, in delusional parasitosis, a fixed false belief of infestation without evidence.

\medterm{Paraskevidekatriaphobia} An excessive fear of Friday the 13th, a specific situational phobia when it causes marked distress, avoidance, or impairment.

\medterm{Parasomnia} A parasomnia is an undesirable movement, behavior, emotion, or experience occurring during sleep or transitions between sleep and wakefulness.

\medterm{Parasuicide} Parasuicide is an older term for a suicide attempt or intentional self-harm without fatal outcome; current assessment focuses on self-harm, intent, safety, and urgent support.

\medterm{Parathyroid gland} Parathyroid gland refers to an anatomical structure, biological substance, or physiologic concept used in describing the human body and its function.

\medterm{Parenchymal} Parenchymal means relating to the functional cells of an organ, distinguished from its supporting connective tissue, vessels, and framework.

\medterm{Parentectomy} Parentectomy is surgical removal of a parent or source structure, such as the parent vessel or lesion, and its precise meaning depends on the procedure.

\medterm{Parenteral} Parenteral means delivered by a route other than the gastrointestinal tract, commonly by injection or infusion such as intravenous, intramuscular, or subcutaneous administration.

\medterm{Parietal} An anatomical structure, tissue, or physiologic process with the location or function indicated by the term; disease or injury changes its expected findings.

\medterm{Parietal pericardium} An anatomical structure, tissue, or physiologic process with the location or function indicated by the term; disease or injury changes its expected findings.

\medterm{Paroxysm} A sudden attack or episodic surge of a symptom, sign, or physiologic activity, such as pain, coughing, arrhythmia, seizure, or autonomic symptoms.

\medterm{Paroxysmal atrial tachycardia} Sudden episodes of rapid, regular atrial rhythm that start and stop abruptly, often producing palpitations, lightheadedness, chest discomfort, or shortness of breath.

\medterm{Partial seizure} A seizure beginning in a localized region of one cerebral hemisphere, now called a focal seizure, with symptoms determined by the involved network and awareness either preserved or impaired.

\medterm{Parturition} The process of childbirth, including cervical ripening, labor contractions, delivery of the fetus, and expulsion of the placenta.

\medterm{PAS} PAS is an abbreviation that may mean periodic-acid–Schiff staining, para-aminosalicylic acid, or pulmonary-artery systolic pressure; context determines the meaning.

\medterm{Passive exercise} Passive exercise is a medical procedure, treatment, device, or care topic; indications, benefits, risks, and follow-up depend on the patient and clinical goal.

\medterm{PAT} PAT is a context-dependent abbreviation that may refer to paroxysmal atrial tachycardia, a rapid rhythm beginning in the atria, or another clinical term.

\medterm{Patellofemoral syndrome (PFS)} A disease, symptom, syndrome, or clinical finding involving the body system or process named by the term; characteristic features, causes, severity, and complications guide diagnosis and management.

\medterm{Patent (adjective)} Patent means open, unobstructed, or functioning, as in a patent airway, vessel, duct, or congenital fetal channel.

\medterm{Patent (noun)} A patent is a legal grant giving an inventor defined rights over an invention for a limited period, subject to applicable law.

\medterm{Patho-} The term Patho- is a medical word element or prefix whose precise meaning depends on the word or clinical context in which it is used.

\medterm{Pathogenic} Pathogenic means capable of causing disease in a susceptible host; pathogenicity depends on the organism or factor, dose, route, and host defenses.

\medterm{Pathognomonic} Pathognomonic means so characteristic of a disease that its presence strongly identifies that disease, although few findings are absolutely diagnostic on their own.

\medterm{Pathologic} Pathologic means related to disease or an abnormal structural or functional change, rather than normal anatomy or physiology.

\medterm{pathy} A suffix meaning disease, disorder, or abnormality, as in neuropathy, cardiomyopathy, and nephropathy.

\medterm{Patient autonomy} A patient’s right and capacity to make informed voluntary decisions about healthcare after receiving understandable information about options, benefits, risks, and alternatives.

\medterm{Pavlov pouch} A Pavlov pouch is a surgically created isolated stomach pouch used in experimental physiology to study gastric secretion and neural control.

\medterm{PCA} PCA is a context-dependent abbreviation, commonly meaning patient-controlled analgesia, in which a patient activates prescribed doses from an infusion device.

\medterm{PCBs} PCBs, or polychlorinated biphenyls, are persistent industrial chemicals that accumulate in tissues and can affect the nervous, endocrine, immune, and reproductive systems.

\medterm{PCO} PCO may mean polycystic ovaries or posterior capsular opacification; the intended condition depends on the clinical context.

\medterm{PCO disease} A disease, symptom, syndrome, or clinical finding involving the body system or process named by the term; characteristic features, causes, severity, and complications guide diagnosis and management.

\medterm{PCT} PCT is a context-dependent abbreviation that may mean procalcitonin, porphyria cutanea tarda, or patient-care technician; the full term should be specified.

\medterm{PDR} PDR commonly means Physicians’ Desk Reference, a drug-information reference, but the abbreviation has other uses and should be interpreted in context.

\medterm{Pediatric} Pediatric means relating to the medical care of infants, children, and adolescents, whose anatomy, physiology, development, and medication needs differ from adults.

\medterm{Pedophobia} An excessive or persistent fear of children or infants that causes distress, avoidance, or impairment and may be classified as a specific phobia when clinically significant.

\medterm{PEEP} PEEP, or positive end-expiratory pressure, is pressure maintained in the airways at the end of exhalation during mechanical ventilation to prevent alveolar collapse.

\medterm{Peeping tom} Peeping tom is a nonclinical term for a person who secretly observes others, often for sexual gratification; assessment focuses on consent, harm, and safety.

\medterm{PEF} PEF, or peak expiratory flow, is the fastest airflow produced during a forceful exhalation and is used chiefly to monitor variable airway obstruction such as asthma.

\medterm{PEG} A medicine or pharmacologic agent used for the condition or pathway indicated by its name; mechanism, dose, interactions, contraindications, and adverse effects depend on the specific product and patient.

\medterm{Peliosis hepatis} Blood-filled cyst-like spaces within the liver, associated with medicines, toxins, infection, malignancy, or systemic disease and occasionally complicated by liver failure or rupture.

\medterm{Pelvic} An anatomical structure, tissue, or physiologic process with the location or function indicated by the term; disease or injury changes its expected findings.

\medterm{Penile} Penile means relating to the penis, the external male organ involved in urination and sexual reproduction.

\medterm{Penis, erection of the} Penile rigidity produced by increased arterial inflow and reduced venous outflow after sexual or reflex stimulation; persistent difficulty is erectile dysfunction.

\medterm{Penis, small} A penis that is substantially below expected stretched length for age, usually assessed as micropenis in a newborn or child after excluding measurement and developmental variation.

\medterm{PEP} PEP is a context-dependent abbreviation, including post-exposure prophylaxis, a preventive medicine course after a possible infection exposure.

\medterm{Percentile} A percentile indicates the percentage of a reference population with a value below a measurement, such as height, weight, or test score.

\medterm{Percutaneous} Percutaneous means performed through the skin, usually with a needle, catheter, wire, or other instrument to diagnose or treat disease.

\medterm{Percutaneous nephrolithotripsy (PNL)} Percutaneous nephrolithotripsy removes or fragments kidney stones through a tract made through the skin into the kidney, usually for larger or complex stones.

\medterm{Perforated Bowel} A perforated bowel is a hole through the intestinal wall allowing contents to leak into the abdomen, causing peritonitis and potentially life-threatening sepsis.

\medterm{Peri-} The term Peri- is a medical word element or prefix whose precise meaning depends on the word or clinical context in which it is used.

\medterm{Periappendiceal} Periappendiceal means located around the appendix, as in inflammation, fluid, an abscess, or fat stranding near the appendiceal tissue.

\medterm{Pericardial} An anatomical structure, tissue, or physiologic process with the location or function indicated by the term; disease or injury changes its expected findings.

\medterm{Perinatal} Perinatal refers to the period around birth, conventionally including late pregnancy and the first days or weeks after delivery depending on the definition used.

\medterm{Perinatal transmission} Transmission of an infection or condition from mother to infant during pregnancy, labor, delivery, or breastfeeding, as with HIV, hepatitis B, syphilis, or certain genetic and congenital disorders.

\medterm{Perinatologist} A perinatologist is an obstetrician specializing in high-risk pregnancy, fetal medicine, prenatal diagnosis, and complications affecting the pregnant person or fetus.

\medterm{Periodic abstinence} Periodic abstinence is avoiding intercourse during estimated fertile days to reduce pregnancy likelihood; cycle timing is imperfect and does not prevent infections.

\medterm{Periodic breathing} Periodic breathing consists of repeated cycles of shallow or absent breathing followed by deeper breaths, occurring in some infants, during sleep, or with neurologic or cardiopulmonary disease.

\medterm{Perionychium} The skin and soft tissue surrounding a fingernail or toenail, including the nail folds and nearby cuticle; infection or inflammation causes paronychia.

\medterm{Perioperative} Perioperative means relating to the period before, during, and after an operation, including preparation, anesthesia, recovery, and complication prevention.

\medterm{Pes} Pes means foot; it is also used in terms describing foot shape or deformity, such as pes planus or pes cavus.

\medterm{Pestilence} Pestilence is an old term for a severe, widespread, often fatal infectious disease outbreak; modern usage usually specifies the pathogen and epidemiologic event.

\medterm{Ph.D.} A Ph.D. is a doctoral research degree awarded after advanced study and an original dissertation; it does not by itself confer a clinical license.

\medterm{Phago-} The term Phago- is a medical word element or prefix whose precise meaning depends on the word or clinical context in which it is used.

\medterm{Phalanx} A phalanx is one of the bones of a finger or toe; each digit has proximal and distal bones, and the thumb has two rather than three.

\medterm{Pharmacologist} A pharmacologist studies how medicines and chemicals act in living systems, including mechanisms, metabolism, toxicity, and therapeutic effects.

\medterm{Pharmacopoeia} A pharmacopoeia is an official reference specifying standards for identity, purity, strength, testing, and preparation of medicines.

\medterm{Phe} Phe is the standard abbreviation for phenylalanine, an essential amino acid incorporated into proteins and metabolized through a pathway disrupted in phenylketonuria.

\medterm{Pheresis} Pheresis is removal of a selected component from blood by an apheresis machine while returning the remaining blood components to the donor or patient.

\medterm{Phlebo- (prefix)} Phlebo- is a medical prefix meaning relating to a vein, as in phlebitis, phlebectomy, or phlebography.

\medterm{PHS} PHS is an abbreviation whose meaning depends on context, including public health service or primary hyperhidrosis; the full term should be specified.

\medterm{Physical child abuse} Physical child abuse is intentional physical injury or harmful force inflicted on a child, requiring immediate safety assessment, medical care, and safeguarding action.

\medterm{Physician} A doctor trained and licensed to diagnose illness, provide medical treatment, prevent disease, and coordinate care within the scope of the relevant specialty and jurisdiction.

\medterm{Physician assistant (PA)} A physician assistant is a licensed clinician who examines, diagnoses, treats, and counsels patients within the scope of local law and a supervising or collaborative practice arrangement.

\medterm{Physicians' Desk Reference} A reference source containing prescribing information, indications, contraindications, dosing, warnings, and adverse effects for medicines and selected products.

\medterm{Physiologic} Physiologic means relating to normal body function or a process occurring within the expected biological range.

\medterm{Physiologic jaundice of the newborn} A disease, symptom, syndrome, or clinical finding involving the body system or process named by the term; characteristic features, causes, severity, and complications guide diagnosis and management.

\medterm{PI} PI is a context-dependent abbreviation, including principal investigator, pulmonary insufficiency, or protease inhibitor; the intended meaning must be specified.

\medterm{Pigmentation} Coloration of skin, hair, eyes, or other tissue produced mainly by melanin, carotenoids, blood, or exogenous material; abnormal changes may reflect inflammation, endocrine disease, drugs, or tumors.

\medterm{Pisa syndrome} A disease, symptom, syndrome, or clinical finding involving the body system or process named by the term; characteristic features, causes, severity, and complications guide diagnosis and management.

\medterm{Placenta, succenturiate} A succenturiate placenta has one or more accessory placental lobes separate from the main placenta, which can cause retained tissue or fetal-vessel complications after delivery.

\medterm{Plan B} Plan B is a levonorgestrel emergency contraceptive intended to reduce pregnancy risk after unprotected intercourse or contraceptive failure; it does not end an established pregnancy.

\medterm{Plant thorn arthritis} Plant thorn arthritis is persistent joint inflammation after a thorn puncture introduces plant material, sometimes requiring removal of foreign material and treatment of infection or inflammation.

\medterm{Plantar} An anatomical structure, tissue, or physiologic process with the location or function indicated by the term; disease or injury changes its expected findings.

\medterm{Plantar response} An anatomical structure, tissue, or physiologic process with the location or function indicated by the term; disease or injury changes its expected findings.

\medterm{Plaque psoriasis} The most common form of psoriasis, producing well-demarcated raised red plaques with silvery scale, commonly on elbows, knees, scalp, and lower back and sometimes associated with arthritis.

\medterm{Plasma osmolarity} Plasma osmolarity is a biochemical, genetic, or chemical term used in medicine; its significance depends on its structure, function, concentration, pathway, or exposure.

\medterm{plegia} -plegia is a suffix meaning paralysis, as in paraplegia, hemiplegia, or quadriplegia.

\medterm{Pleiotropic cytokine} A cytokine that acts on multiple cell types or produces several biological effects, such as changes in inflammation, blood formation, immunity, or tissue repair.

\medterm{Pleomorphic} Pleomorphic means having multiple forms or marked variation in cell or tissue appearance; in pathology, it may be a feature of malignancy but is not diagnostic alone.

\medterm{Plethoric} Plethoric means unusually full, congested, or ruddy, often describing the appearance associated with increased blood volume or red-cell mass.

\medterm{Pleural} An anatomical structure, tissue, or physiologic process with the location or function indicated by the term; disease or injury changes its expected findings.

\medterm{PLP} PLP commonly means pyridoxal 5′-phosphate, the active coenzyme form of vitamin B6 required by many enzymes, but the abbreviation has other uses.

\medterm{PMD} PMD is a context-dependent abbreviation, including Pelizaeus–Merzbacher disease, a rare inherited disorder of central myelination, or a professional medical designation.

\medterm{PMDD} Premenstrual dysphoric disorder is a severe cyclic mood disorder occurring before menstruation with irritability, depression, anxiety, and functional impairment that improve after menses.

\medterm{PML} PML commonly means progressive multifocal leukoencephalopathy, a JC-virus demyelinating brain infection during severe immune suppression, but it may have other meanings.

\medterm{Pneumo-} The term Pneumo- is a medical word element or prefix whose precise meaning depends on the word or clinical context in which it is used.

\medterm{Pneumocyte} A disease, symptom, syndrome, or clinical finding involving the body system or process named by the term; characteristic features, causes, severity, and complications guide diagnosis and management.

\medterm{Pneumonia, bilateral} A disease, symptom, syndrome, or clinical finding involving the body system or process named by the term; characteristic features, causes, severity, and complications guide diagnosis and management.

\medterm{PNS (peripheral nervous system)} The peripheral nervous system includes nerves and ganglia outside the brain and spinal cord and carries sensory, motor, and autonomic signals between the body and central nervous system.

\medterm{Poison} A poison is a substance capable of causing injury, illness, or death when swallowed, inhaled, injected, or absorbed in a sufficient dose.

\medterm{Poly-} The term Poly- is a medical word element or prefix whose precise meaning depends on the word or clinical context in which it is used.

\medterm{Polycystic ovarian syndrome} A disease, symptom, syndrome, or clinical finding involving the body system or process named by the term; characteristic features, causes, severity, and complications guide diagnosis and management.

\medterm{Polycythemia vera (PV)} Polycythemia vera is a myeloproliferative blood cancer, usually driven by a JAK2 mutation, causing excess red cells and increased risks of thrombosis, bleeding, and transformation.

\medterm{Polygenic disease} A disease, symptom, syndrome, or clinical finding involving the body system or process named by the term; characteristic features, causes, severity, and complications guide diagnosis and management.

\medterm{Polyglandular autoimmune syndrome (PGA)} An anatomical structure, tissue, or physiologic process with the location or function indicated by the term; disease or injury changes its expected findings.

\medterm{Polymorphic} Polymorphic is a biochemical, genetic, or chemical term used in medicine; its significance depends on its structure, function, concentration, pathway, or exposure.

\medterm{Polymorphism} Polymorphism is a biochemical, genetic, or chemical term used in medicine; its significance depends on its structure, function, concentration, pathway, or exposure.

\medterm{Polymorphonuclear leukocyte} A white blood cell with a multilobed nucleus, especially a neutrophil, eosinophil, or basophil, involved in innate immune defense and inflammation.

\medterm{Polymyalgia rheumatica (PMR)} Polymyalgia rheumatica causes abrupt bilateral shoulder and hip-girdle aching and stiffness in older adults and can occur with giant-cell arteritis.

\medterm{Polyneuritis, acute idiopathic} Polyneuritis, acute idiopathic is a biochemical, genetic, or chemical term used in medicine; its significance depends on its structure, function, concentration, pathway, or exposure.

\medterm{PON} PON is a context-dependent abbreviation that may refer to postoperative nausea, paraoxonase, or another clinical term; the full meaning should be stated.

\medterm{Poor circulation} Reduced or abnormal blood flow, usually referring to arterial insufficiency or venous disease, causing coolness, color change, pain, swelling, ulcers, or delayed wound healing.

\medterm{Popliteal} Popliteal means relating to the hollow behind the knee, where major nerves, blood vessels, lymph nodes, and tendons pass.

\medterm{Porcine} Porcine means derived from or relating to pigs; porcine tissues, enzymes, and biologic products may be used in medicine after appropriate processing and screening.

\medterm{Positive-strand RNA virus} A positive-strand RNA virus has a genome that can function directly as messenger RNA in host cells, allowing translation soon after infection.

\medterm{Post} Post- is a prefix meaning after, as in post-operative, postpartum, or postprandial.

\medterm{Posteroanterior} Posteroanterior means directed from back to front; a PA chest radiograph uses this projection to reduce magnification of the heart.

\medterm{Posthitis} Inflammation of the foreskin, causing redness, swelling, itching, pain, discharge, or difficulty retracting it; infection, irritation, diabetes, and poor hygiene are possible contributors.

\medterm{Postmenopausal} Postmenopausal describes the period after menopause, when ovarian estrogen production and menstrual periods have permanently ceased.

\medterm{Postop} Postop is an abbreviation for postoperative, meaning relating to the period after a surgical procedure.

\medterm{Postoperative} Postoperative means occurring or provided after surgery, including recovery, wound care, pain control, rehabilitation, and complication monitoring.

\medterm{Postprandial} Postprandial means occurring after a meal, especially the changes in glucose, insulin, digestion, and circulation after eating.

\medterm{Postural} Postural means relating to body position or posture, including symptoms or vital signs that change when a person sits, stands, or lies down.

\medterm{Posture} Posture is the alignment and positioning of the body during sitting, standing, movement, or rest and depends on bones, joints, muscles, balance, and nervous control.

\medterm{Pott's disease} A disease, symptom, syndrome, or clinical finding involving the body system or process named by the term; characteristic features, causes, severity, and complications guide diagnosis and management.

\medterm{Pouch of Douglas} The pouch of Douglas is the peritoneal recess between the uterus and rectum, also called the rectouterine pouch, where fluid may collect.

\medterm{Power outage food safety} Power outage food safety is a procedure, device, treatment, or care intervention; indications, benefits, risks, and follow-up depend on the patient and clinical goal.

\medterm{PPD} PPD may mean purified protein derivative used in tuberculosis skin testing or post-partum depression; context determines the intended meaning.

\medterm{Prandial} Prandial means relating to a meal, as in preprandial before eating or postprandial after eating.

\medterm{Precordial catch syndrome (PCS)} A disease, symptom, syndrome, or clinical finding involving the body system or process named by the term; characteristic features, causes, severity, and complications guide diagnosis and management.

\medterm{Predispose} To predispose is to increase a person’s susceptibility or likelihood of developing a disease or response when additional triggers occur.

\medterm{Prefixes, medical} Word beginnings that modify medical terms by indicating location, number, time, speed, size, direction, deficiency, excess, or other meaning, such as hyper-, hypo-, peri-, and brady-.

\medterm{Pregnant} Pregnant means carrying a developing embryo or fetus in the uterus after conception; pregnancy status affects medicines, imaging, and clinical decisions.

\medterm{Premature baby} An infant born before 37 completed weeks of pregnancy, with risks related to immature lungs, brain, feeding, temperature control, immunity, and other organ systems.

\medterm{Premenstrual} Occurring during the days before menstruation; the term commonly refers to cyclical symptoms such as mood change, breast tenderness, bloating, headache, or fatigue.

\medterm{Premie} Premie is an informal term for a premature infant, born before 37 completed weeks of gestation and at risk for complications related to immature organs.

\medterm{Prenatal} Prenatal means occurring during pregnancy before birth, including care, testing, development, counseling, and treatment of the pregnant person or fetus.

\medterm{Prenatal development} Prenatal development is growth and differentiation of the embryo and fetus from conception to birth, influenced by genes, nutrition, maternal health, and exposures.

\medterm{Preop} Preop is an abbreviation for preoperative, meaning relating to preparation and assessment before surgery.

\medterm{Prepubertal} Prepubertal means before the physical and hormonal changes of puberty have begun or progressed to reproductive maturity.

\medterm{Pressor} A pressor agent raises blood pressure, usually by constricting blood vessels, increasing heart activity, or both.

\medterm{Primary} Primary means first, original, or arising independently rather than secondarily from another condition; its precise meaning depends on the clinical context.

\medterm{Primary immunodeficiency disease} A disease, symptom, syndrome, or clinical finding involving the body system or process named by the term; characteristic features, causes, severity, and complications guide diagnosis and management.

\medterm{Private mutation} A genetic variant confined to a family, individual, tumor, or small population rather than commonly seen in the general population; its significance requires genetic and clinical interpretation.

\medterm{Pro-} The term Pro- is a medical word element or prefix whose precise meaning depends on the word or clinical context in which it is used.

\medterm{Probiotics} Probiotics are live microorganisms that may provide a health benefit when consumed in adequate amounts, although effects are strain-specific and vary by condition.

\medterm{PROC} PROC is the gene symbol for protein C, an anticoagulant protein that limits clot formation; inherited deficiency increases thrombosis risk.

\medterm{Process} A process is an ordered series of biological, physical, chemical, or operational changes that produces a result.

\medterm{Proclivity} Proclivity is a tendency or predisposition toward a particular behavior, response, disease, or outcome.

\medterm{Progeny} Progeny means offspring or descendants produced by an organism, individual, or breeding line.

\medterm{Progressive} Progressive means increasing, spreading, or worsening over time, although the rate and reversibility depend on the disease or process.

\medterm{Proinflammatory} Proinflammatory describes a substance, cell, or response that promotes inflammation through mediators such as cytokines, chemokines, or lipid signals.

\medterm{Proliferative} Proliferative means involving increased cell multiplication or tissue growth, which may be normal, reactive, benign, or malignant.

\medterm{Prophylactic} Prophylactic means intended to prevent disease, infection, injury, or another unwanted outcome before it occurs.

\medterm{Propylthiouracil (PTU)} Propylthiouracil is an antithyroid drug that reduces thyroid-hormone synthesis and conversion of T4 to T3; liver injury and fetal risks require careful use.

\medterm{Prostaglandin E2} A prostaglandin that mediates inflammation, pain, fever, vasodilation, gastric protection, cervical ripening, and uterine contraction and is used in selected obstetric and cardiovascular treatments.

\medterm{Prostaglandin F2-alpha} A prostaglandin that promotes smooth-muscle contraction and luteolysis; analogues are used in glaucoma, postpartum hemorrhage, labor-related care, and veterinary medicine.

\medterm{Prostate enlargement} An anatomical structure, tissue, or physiologic process with the location or function indicated by the term; disease or injury changes its expected findings.

\medterm{Prostate gland} Prostate gland refers to an anatomical structure, biological substance, or physiologic concept used in describing the human body and its function.

\medterm{Prostatodynia} Prostatodynia is an older term for pelvic or prostate-region pain without proven bacterial infection, now often considered within chronic pelvic pain syndrome.

\medterm{Prosthetic} Prosthetic means relating to an artificial replacement or device that restores, supports, or substitutes for a body part or function.

\medterm{Protean} Protean is a biochemical, genetic, or chemical term used in medicine; its significance depends on its structure, function, concentration, pathway, or exposure.

\medterm{Protease} Protease is a biochemical, genetic, or chemical term used in medicine; its significance depends on its structure, function, concentration, pathway, or exposure.

\medterm{Protein malnutrition} Protein malnutrition refers to an anatomical structure, biological substance, or physiologic concept used in describing the human body and its function.

\medterm{Proteolipid protein} Proteolipid protein refers to an anatomical structure, biological substance, or physiologic concept used in describing the human body and its function.

\medterm{Pruritic} Itchy or causing an urge to scratch; pruritus can result from skin inflammation, allergy, infection, systemic disease, medications, or neuropathic mechanisms.

\medterm{Pseudomonas aeruginosa} Pseudomonas aeruginosa is a biological organism or infectious agent; its clinical importance depends on the species, host, site, exposure, and evidence of infection or colonization.

\medterm{Pseudotumor cerebri} An older term for idiopathic intracranial hypertension, in which raised pressure around the brain causes headache, transient visual obscurations, pulsatile tinnitus, and possible papilledema without a mass lesion.

\medterm{PSP} PSP commonly means progressive supranuclear palsy, a neurodegenerative disorder causing early postural instability, vertical gaze impairment, rigidity, and cognitive change.

\medterm{Psychiatric} Relating to the assessment, diagnosis, treatment, or prevention of disorders of mood, thought, perception, behavior, or emotional functioning.

\medterm{Psycho-oncology} The clinical and research field addressing psychological, social, behavioral, and communication needs of people with cancer and their families across diagnosis, treatment, survivorship, and end of life.

\medterm{Psychologist, clinical} A licensed mental-health professional who assesses and treats psychological, emotional, behavioral, and cognitive problems using psychotherapy and other evidence-based interventions within local scope of practice.

\medterm{Psychotropic medication} Psychotropic medication is a medicine, biologic, or pharmacologic term; its use, dose, interactions, and adverse effects depend on the specific clinical indication.

\medterm{PTC (percutaneous transhepatic cholangiography)} PTC is imaging of the bile ducts after contrast is injected through a needle passed through the skin and liver; drainage or stenting may follow.

\medterm{PTU} PTU is the abbreviation for propylthiouracil, an antithyroid medicine used selectively because of liver toxicity and other safety considerations.

\medterm{Pulmonary medicine} An anatomical structure, tissue, or physiologic process with the location or function indicated by the term; disease or injury changes its expected findings.

\medterm{Pump-oxygenator} Pump-oxygenator is a biochemical, genetic, or chemical term used in medicine; its significance depends on its structure, function, concentration, pathway, or exposure.

\medterm{Pustulosis} A skin disorder characterized by sterile or infectious pustules, which may be localized or widespread and associated with inflammation, fever, psoriasis, drug reactions, or infection.

\medterm{Putrid throat} Putrid throat refers to an anatomical structure, biological substance, or physiologic concept used in describing the human body and its function.

\medterm{PUVA} PUVA combines a psoralen medicine with ultraviolet-A exposure to treat selected skin diseases; eye protection and cancer-risk precautions are required.

\medterm{PV} PV is a context-dependent abbreviation, including polycythemia vera, plasma volume, or peripheral vision; the full term should be specified.

\medterm{PVB} PVB is a context-dependent abbreviation that may mean paravertebral block, a regional anesthetic technique, or another medical term.

\medterm{PVC} A PVC, or premature ventricular contraction, is an early heartbeat arising in a ventricle; occasional PVCs are common, while frequent or symptomatic beats need evaluation.

\medterm{PX} PX is a context-dependent abbreviation that may mean prognosis, patient, prescription, or a genetic marker; clinical documentation should spell out the intended term.

\medterm{Pyarthrosis} Infection of a joint with pus in the joint space, usually bacterial septic arthritis, causing acute pain, swelling, restricted movement, fever, and risk of cartilage destruction.

\medterm{Pyelo} Pyelo is an informal shortening usually referring to pyelonephritis, an infection of the kidney and renal pelvis that can cause fever and flank pain.

\medterm{Pyrimidine} Pyrimidine is a biochemical, genetic, or chemical term used in medicine; its significance depends on its structure, function, concentration, pathway, or exposure.

\medterm{Pyrosis} Heartburn, a burning discomfort behind the breastbone caused most often by reflux of stomach contents into the esophagus.

\medterm{PYY} PYY, or peptide YY, is a gut hormone released after eating that reduces appetite and slows gastrointestinal motility.

\medterm{Peyer's Patches} Organized lymphoid tissue in the mucosa and submucosa of the ileum. These aggregates sample intestinal contents and contribute to immune surveillance of the gastrointestinal tract.

\synonyms
ileal lymphoid nodules

\medterm{Pulmonary Arteries} The paired arteries that carry deoxygenated blood from the right ventricle to the lungs for gas exchange.

\synonyms
pulmonary artery

\medterm{Pet allergy} An allergic reaction to proteins in an animal's skin flakes, saliva, or urine, commonly causing rhinitis, conjunctivitis, or asthma.

\medterm{Popliteal artery aneurysm} An abnormal enlargement of the artery behind the knee that may cause limb ischemia, clotting, or embolization.

\medterm{Primary progressive aphasia} A neurodegenerative disorder causing gradual worsening of language abilities, including speaking, naming, comprehension, reading, or writing.

\medterm{Paraneoplastic syndromes of the nervous system} Neurologic disorders caused by an immune response to cancer rather than by direct tumor invasion or metastasis.

\medterm{Pinched nerve} Compression or irritation of a nerve that can cause pain, tingling, numbness, or weakness along the nerve's distribution.

\medterm{Pink eye (conjunctivitis)} Inflammation or infection of the conjunctiva causing eye redness, discharge, irritation, itching, or tearing.

\medterm{Pubic lice (crabs)} Infestation of coarse body hair by lice, usually causing intense itching and requiring treatment and contact precautions.

\medterm{Painful intercourse (dyspareunia)} Persistent or recurrent genital pain before, during, or after sexual intercourse, with physical, hormonal, emotional, or relationship-related causes.

\medterm{Premature ventricular contractions (PVCs)} Early extra heartbeats originating in the heart's ventricles, often felt as fluttering, skipped beats, or pounding and frequently benign.

\medterm{Pre-hepatic Jaundice (Haemolytic Jaundice)} Jaundice caused by excessive breakdown of red blood cells before bilirubin reaches the liver, producing increased unconjugated bilirubin.

\synonyms
Haemolytic Jaundice

\medterm{Post-hepatic Jaundice (Obstructive Jaundice)} Jaundice caused by obstruction of bile flow after bilirubin has been processed by the liver, commonly from gallstones, strictures, or tumours.

\synonyms
Obstructive Jaundice

\medterm{Premature Labour (Preterm Labour)} Labour that begins before 37 completed weeks of pregnancy and may result in preterm birth.

\synonyms
Preterm Labour

\medterm{Precipitate Labour} Unusually rapid labour, often defined as delivery within three hours of the onset of regular contractions.

\medterm{Plane Warts (Verruca Plana)} Small, smooth, relatively flat warts caused by HPV, often occurring in groups on the face, hands, or legs.

\synonyms
Verruca Plana

\medterm{Plantar Warts (Verruca Plantaris)} Warts on the soles of the feet caused by HPV. Pressure may push them inward and produce pain during walking.

\synonyms
Verruca Plantaris
